# Cerid master plan

<!-- doc-role: tracker -->
> Only live slice/finding tracker. Current work: [ROADMAP](ROADMAP.md); current rules: [AGENTS](../AGENTS.md).

**This is the only live slice/subslice tracker.** Updated 2026-09-14 with the DIAG programme.
Current focus is the single pointer in [context](../context.md); this table alone owns live state.
The last shipped execution milestone is [CEIR-35](sessions/2026-09-11-ceir-35z-band-close.md).
Agent entry: [START_HERE](../START_HERE.md). Broad architecture/gaps:
[whole-system review](research/2026-09-12-cerid-whole-system-review.md); every G01–G30 finding has owners in this table.

## Purpose

Build the fully authorable, performant, cross-API renderer; the complete Cerid-owned UI; and **CR-D007**, the game
and engineering application editor. Algorithms, shaders, pipelines, frame graphs and behaviour are editable assets:
CHIR authors high-level behaviour, CEIR executes, CKIR expresses device kernels. The editor is a consumer of public
modules. Standalone games, engineering apps, the notebook and later DAWs use those same modules.

## Confirmed order and decisions

Recorded in [ADR-0129](decisions/0129-renderer-ui-editor-delivery-order.md).

**2026-09-14 user addition:** immediately after REPO.DEV, complete the **DIAG** runtime diagnostic foundation,
then the retained repository close and renderer sequence below. [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md)
and the [DIAG contract](design/runtime-diagnostics.md) define all children; the [research](research/2026-09-14-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md)
maps DG01–DG18 source findings. Existing CI/review gates remain. MAC.DIAG/WEB.DIAG qualify later-platform integration;
the early foundation must not depend on a future editor or claim universal bug detection.

1. Complete the **entire retained renderer feature library**, including virtual geometry, hybrid GI, neural rendering
   dependencies and OFF-1…9 production offline rendering. This is the user's explicit choice on 2026-09-12.
2. Build full `crd-ui` and CR-D007. Bootstrap the shell at I2D-4, then build the remaining widgets within it.
3. Finish hesap GPU and the MATLAB-like notebook, as a CR-D007 workspace **and** a thin standalone host.
4. Resume media, audio/plugins, later applications and preserved engine/domain work. Windows/Linux qualify first;
   macOS and web follow explicitly. Physics/geometry and unrelated code are untouched by this documentation audit.

The expanded AI programme qualifies **training and inference together**, including scoped full training, fine-tuning,
distillation and specialized game/product agents. Neural-renderer dependencies qualify before UI. Collaboration uses
an authoritative project host with local/offline recovery; multiplayer has a separate authoritative simulation contract.
[ADR-0130](decisions/0130-system-qualification-and-agent-driven-products.md) distinguishes confirmed product directions
from proposed mechanism choices. Broader modeling, DAW, CAD/CAM/EDA, embedded, digital-twin and hardware work remains
in the same sequence with explicit qualification children; no retained ID was removed.

Renderer `.runtime` rows finish the full runtime/asset work before UI. `.authoring` children then prove those same
features in CR-D007; the original family ID closes only afterward. This preserves the visual-authoring requirement
without pretending an editor exists before UI. Media codec IDs remain MED-1…12; MED-GPU-* identifies the separate
architecture contracts that formerly reused the same numbers.

**Still to review before implementation:** RAH-0's refreshed no-loss design and ADR-0107's full proposal. The language,
service and qualification recommendations are concrete in the execution contract. The audit request authorizes
documentation changes, not an invented ADR acceptance. No unavailable advisor sign-off is claimed. The old CEIR loop
grant ended with CEIR-35; this table does not silently reinstate it.

## How to use this document

Read [context](../context.md) for the current pointer, then this table. Open only the row's detail and ADR for work.
The [execution contract](design/renderer-ui-execution-contract.md) defines ownership, quality, publishing and UI gates.
The [inherited catalogue](design/rendering-ui-contracts.md) contains the complete feature lists; a short table cell
does not reduce them. The [audit](research/2026-09-12-system-audit.md) contains findings, evidence and module inventory.
[Systems](systems/README.md) maps the code; [decisions](decisions/README.md) records architecture.

**States:** Done = complete contract with evidence and sequential acceptance; In progress = the sole current row;
Blocked = named unmet non-CI gate; Needs CI = available local work finished, published CI output still required;
Recorded = dated evidence/documentation, not a new test run; Review = unresolved census/decision;
Open = unimplemented/unqualified contract; Partial = retained work/evidence awaiting full sequential acceptance;
Later = retained outside the current
renderer/UI/science delivery. Source plans and archives never carry live status. Capability maturity is a separate
machine-derived fact in [the registry](capabilities/gpu-platform-capabilities.toml), not inferred from these states.

Query `python scripts/check-master-plan.py --slice RAH-1`, `--find A24`, or `--next` for a small view of this same table.
Finding IDs link directly to evidence; the linked owner rows carry their live state. No second bug backlog is allowed.

## Strict sequential execution

**Latest user direction, 2026-09-12:** start at the earliest unfinished work unless explicitly directed to a future
slice. Leave no gaps. If evidence needs CI output, say so and continue available work. This supersedes the earlier
same-day rule that stopped all advancement while CI waited; other scope, hardware and renderer review gates remain.

1. Walk the Order column. Done and historical Recorded rows require no implementation; Needs CI rows retain an
   external verification gate. The first remaining row is the default work position, including Open, Partial, Review
   and Blocked. Context points there; only that row may be In progress. Finish all available work in that row first.
2. **Needs CI is not Done.** Use it only when all presently available local work is finished and published CI output
   is the remaining evidence. Link the actual run and dated local proof in the row. Say "needs CI output" and continue
   the next available work; never drop its requirements, weaken an oracle or claim an unobserved pass. Inspect changed
   CI results first on re-entry: a real failure returns to its owning row and takes priority in table order.
3. An explicit user request to work on a future slice overrides default scheduling, not its prerequisites/quality.
   Record the exact user direction in a session and link it in that row as `[User direction](sessions/<record>.md)`
   with `<!-- user-order: sessions/<record>.md -->`. This marker may be added only for a real explicit instruction,
   never inferred from a quality goal, old grant or agent-written plan. Return to earliest work after that assignment.
4. All full contracts, children, tests, publication/provider evidence and documentation remain required for Done.
   A pending prerequisite is not waived by continued work. Parents close only when every child and inherited gate
   closes. Hardware/design/scope blockers remain binding unless the user explicitly changes their contract.
5. Add a proven missing prerequisite before its owning unfinished row, with references and rationale. Never move
   unrelated work forward, reorder the table to conceal a gap or reclassify unfinished implementation as Recorded.
   New findings keep owning rows and exact evidence; CI waits and parked work are visible in this same table.
6. Previously parked later work remains Partial with its implementation/evidence intact. At its turn audit the full
   contract; reuse valid proof and run missing or newly justified checks. The validator enforces the default order,
   one active position, referenced user overrides and explicitly retained CI waits. No second status queue is allowed.

The [initial order correction](sessions/2026-09-12-strict-slice-order.md) preserves earlier evidence; the
[CI continuation amendment](sessions/2026-09-12-ci-continuation-and-dx12-workloads.md) records the latest direction.
New child IDs are proposed execution decomposition, clearly distinct from original source IDs. Add every additional
child here before implementing it; never create another tracker. A large row starts with a detailed reuse/design/
test note linked from its contract cell. Parent completion requires all children and inherited requirements.

| 051 | <a id="slice-repo.dev.5"></a>**REPO.DEV.5** | Done | Three-tier CI with one coverage contract: `.github/ci-tiers.json` gives every visible preset an owner (validated at configure, in preflight and by the tooling tests); preflight resolves the tier from the changed paths (documentation → preflight, sources → change, build system/workflow/scripts/register or an unknown base → complete; nightly and manual runs → complete) and every heavy job takes its matrix from that resolution; `required` is the one stable status and fails on a skipped expected job or a failed preflight; cancellation is limited to superseded pull-request runs; every lane records its CTest inventory, JUnit, the two-sided register gate and a `conclusion.json` bundle with revision, tree, toolchain and adapter identity. The five never-hosted presets (`win-relwithdebinfo`, `win-debug-scalar`, `win-shipping-profile`, `linux-gcc-debug-scalar`, native `win-vs`) join the complete tier. Bounded CTest parallelism and shards wait for the first complete-tier durations. The introducing push resolves to the complete tier. **Hosted (2026-10-06 audit):** the first all-green complete-tier run, 37311671924 at `4737877d` (25 of 25 jobs): preflight resolved to complete, `required` green with every expected job, an evidence bundle per lane (`conclusion.json` with revision, tree, toolchain and adapter), and the five never-hosted presets plus the native solution building and testing. | [REPO.DEV.4](#slice-repo.dev.4) | [Detail](design/ci-tiers.md); [Research](research/2026-09-12-large-cpp-development-and-ci.md#ci-design); [ADR-0001](decisions/0001-build-language.md); [Evidence](sessions/2026-09-14-ci-tiers.md); [Published CI](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/34780682504); [Repair](sessions/2026-09-14-first-complete-tier-run-repairs.md); [Hosted run 2026-10-05](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37311671924); [Needs CI audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md) |
| 052 | <a id="slice-repo.dev.6"></a>**REPO.DEV.6** | Done | One registry (`cmake/pins.json`) names every external input with version, commit-addressed source, SHA-256 and license: the nine CPM packages acquire as verified archives (`crd_add_pinned_package`, `CRD_INPUT_ARCHIVES` for verified offline copies), the CPM bootstrap, Vulkan SDK, Vulkan headers, SPIRV-Reflect, WARP and actionlint acquire through helpers that download to a partial file and verify before the final name exists, the imgui Vulkan patch applies to a build-owned copy from a versioned spec (the shared cache stays pristine, a drifted anchor fails the configure), every workflow action is a commit with its tag, every runner an explicit image, every checkout `persist-credentials: false`; `check-pins.py` guards the registry at configure, in preflight and in the tooling tests. Local proof: the full `win-debug` graph unchanged except the patched path, Linux reconfigured, four consumers on one source cache with the tree hash identical after the third and the fourth; hosted lanes on the next push. **Hosted (2026-10-06 audit):** not yet shown. Run 37311671924 verified the Vulkan headers, the validation layer and sccache on download, but every lane restored its CPM archive cache (and Windows its Vulkan SDK cache) through the restore-key fallback, as did the first complete run; cold acquisition of every archive, the Vulkan SDK and WARP through the verifying helpers has never run hosted. Needs one cache-cold hosted run. **Cold lanes landed (2026-10-06, user decision: a standing step, option 2):** the complete-tier `win-debug-scalar` and `linux-gcc-debug-scalar` skip every archive cache (`CRD_COLD_PRESETS`); `check-cold-acquisition.py` fails unless the CPM cache starts empty and every package the configure added is pinned and acquired (the Windows lane also installs the Vulkan SDK and the pinned WARP); proven by a real cold configure on the WSL reference host and its tooling test. The next complete-tier run is the hosted proof. **Hosted (2026-10-06, complete run 37395083192):** both cold lanes started from an empty cache. Each acquired all seven packages any configuration selects (Catch2, glfw, zstd, stb, cgltf, MikkTSpace, imgui), verified each against its pinned SHA-256, and passed its whole test lane. The Windows lane also installed the Vulkan SDK 1.4.341.1 and WARP 1.0.20 cold through their verifying helpers. Not yet shown: the two gated pins (Eigen and OpenBLAS, `CRD_BUILD_HESAP_VS_REFERENCE`, which no hosted preset builds), named but never acquired. Rather than leave them unproven, both cold lanes now also download and verify every unselected pin's archive (`check-cold-acquisition.py --fetch-unselected`, through `pins.download`). Both archives verify on the reference host. The next complete-tier run shows the remaining clause. (Count corrected from ten to nine: `tomlplusplus` left with the native toml module, `94856a16`.) **Done (2026-10-06):** the complete-tier [nightly 37445373995](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37445373995) at `41f9962c` (26 of 26 green) shows the last clause. | 064 | <a id="slice-diag.3a"></a>**DIAG.3a** | Done | Complete every current allocator contract: checked size/alignment/range arithmetic, OOM/realloc and ownership; DG02/DG03. Core landed on win-debug (`crd/memory/checked_math.hpp`): constexpr overflow-checked `checked_add`/`checked_mul`/`checked_array_size`/`checked_align_up`/`checked_page_count`/`checked_padded_array_size`, each reporting overflow rather than wrapping (align-up refuses the near-SIZE_MAX wrap the naive form suffers; non-power-of-two alignment rejected). 31 assertions incl. compile-time static_asserts + boundary overflow. Now WIRED into real size paths: `PoolAllocator` slot-pad × count (CRD_FATAL on overflow, always-on) and `LinearAllocator` offset arithmetic (overflow fails like exhaustion, preserving the prior allocation) — contract test proves the near-SIZE_MAX request returns nullptr and the next in-bounds allocation still succeeds. Remaining (next iterations): the remaining allocators' size paths + the full per-allocator zero-size/exhaustion/realloc-failure sweep. **2026-10-06 audit:** reclassified from Needs CI, because the row's own remaining work (the other allocators' size paths and the full per-allocator sweep) is local work. **2026-10-06 boundary sweep:** one contract across all 12 `IAllocator`s (huge sizes and a 2^62 alignment must return nullptr from `try_allocate`, earlier blocks keep their bytes, foreign pointers are not owned) found wrap or assert defects in 9 (the POSIX malloc wrap only on Linux). Fixed with checked arithmetic in TLSF, growable TLSF, growable linear, POSIX malloc, the streaming budget check and the diagnostic decorator's redzone sizing, plus non-fatal `try_allocate` overrides on pool, growable pool and the decorator. The memory test exe sets the sanitizer default `allocator_may_return_null=1` (libc's contract; detection unchanged). All 12 pass on win-debug, win-shipping, win-clang-cl-shipping and win-asan, and on WSL clang TSan, gcc debug and gcc ASan; teeth were verified. **Part 2 (same day):** zero-size `try_allocate` returns nullptr on all 12 (the default `try_allocate` now refuses before `allocate`'s assert). Exhaustion refuses and keeps earlier blocks (linear, stack, pool, TLSF, virtual memory). A failed `reallocate` keeps the old block (the default `reallocate` copied into nullptr and freed it). TLSF `reallocate(p, 64, SIZE_MAX)` wrapped to 0 and split the live block; it now reaches the out-of-memory fatal, which the new `crd-diag-alloc-fatal-specimen` proves as a bounded child in three modes. Teeth were verified for all three fixes. **Part 3:** `OffsetAllocator` refuses a split with an exhausted node pool (it overran `m_free_nodes` in release builds) and refuses foreign, stale or double-freed handles in every build (`free` indexed `m_nodes` unchecked). `RingAllocator::try_claim` refuses an alignment the buffer cannot honour. Teeth were verified. **Part 4:** a block freed into the wrong allocator, over every ordered pair of the 12, is refused (pool, growable pool and TLSF used to adopt it in release builds, handing foreign memory out again), ignored by the arenas, or reported by the decorator; `MallocAllocator` cannot tell (documented limit). TLSF also refuses an immediate double free. Child arenas, nested chains and moved growable pools return exactly what they took from a decorated parent; arenas over external buffers stay inside them. Partial construction: owning arenas whose parent refuses now reach their own fatal instead of building on null (six specimen modes), the growable pool refuses a page non-fatally, and `construct`/`construct_array` handle refusal, count overflow and throwing constructors. Teeth were verified; 153 memory cases pass on win-debug, win-asan and the three WSL lanes (152 on both shipping lanes). Remaining: a hosted run showing the memory suite green on every lane, including the complete tier's `linux-clang-tsan`. **Done (2026-10-09):** the memory suite (162 cases) is green on all 20 test lanes, including the complete tier's `linux-clang-tsan`, in complete runs [37852297628](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37852297628) (push) and [37915366121](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37915366121) (nightly) at `6bd1ba59`; [audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md#rows-closed-on-6bd1ba59-2026-10-09). | [DIAG.2b](#slice-diag.2b) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-3a); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Session](sessions/2026-09-14-diag-2-3-events-recorder-checked-math.md); [Published CI](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/34880793407); [Hosted run 2026-10-05](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37311671924); [Needs CI audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md); [Boundary sweep](sessions/2026-10-06-diag-3a-allocator-boundaries.md); [Complete run 2026-10-09](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37852297628) |

| 071 | <a id="slice-diag.4b"></a>**DIAG.4b** | Done | Controlled schedule replay, bounded weak-memory models and real multicore stress with minimized failing schedules; DG06/DG15. Landed as a compile-time-gated twin (`CRD_JOBS_SCHED_CHECK`; a separate `crd-jobs-schedcheck` library + `crd-jobs-schedcheck-tests` exe, never in CI, zero cost in production): five `CRD_JOBS_SCHED_POINT` yield points on the counter publication/reclamation path, a `SchedOracle` with recording + blocking drivers, scripted schedule replay, greedy delta-minimization of a failing schedule, and a gate-only violation detector proven both ways (repaired = zero false positives; a broken-handshake break switch is FOUND and replayed from its minimized script). Weak-memory coverage is a standalone C11 GenMC model (`tests/support/diag/models/counter_publish_reclaim.c`) with native UAF + generation-assert oracles, checked by the pinned GenMC (see 2026-10-10 below). The hardware regime is a perturbed multicore park/reclaim stress (`test_sched_check.cpp` case 6: 2000×32 roots, 4 threads, seeded ~1/4 yield jitter, 20 s watchdog + assert platform handler so any failure is a bounded exit not a modal-dialog hang). That perturbation surfaced a real production concurrency bug — `jobs::wait()` could `release()` a counter after `counter_decrement`'s `fetch_sub` hit zero but before its `waiters.exchange`, letting a concurrent `acquire` recycle the slot and the stale drain steal the next generation's waiter (premature wake → early release → decrement past zero) — fixed by a `Counter::drained` gate on release (mirrors the `park_finalized` handshake); the stress is now the regression guard. Verified: twin harness 6 cases/45 assertions; perturbed stress 250× standalone + 30× in-harness with zero asserts/hangs; gate-off `crd-jobs-tests` 121 cases/29502 assertions green; `ctest --preset win-asan -R jobs` 28/28; both validators PASS. **2026-10-09 audit:** reclassified to Partial: the GenMC model had never been run and no GenMC was pinned ([audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md#rows-closed-on-6bd1ba59-2026-10-09)). **2026-10-10 user decision:** a GenMC CI lane in the complete/nightly tier, like the TSan lane; if GenMC cannot be installed in CI in reasonable time, fall back to the recorded WSL run as declared evidence. **2026-10-10:** GenMC 0.19.0 pinned (`tools/genmc`, commit-addressed archive, built against apt LLVM 20 by `install-genmc.py`); measured on the WSL reference host in a 4-CPU ubuntu:24.04 container: apt 30 s, build 69 s, model under 1 s, so the lane was added (`linux-gcc` job, `linux-clang-tsan` entry, cached per pin and LLVM; `check-genmc-models.py`; declared under `preset_steps` in ci-tiers.json). The first run found a second reclamation race in the engine: `counter_decrement` stored `drained` before its drain loop, so an ABA-cancelled fiber could pass the gate and unwind its Waiter while the decrement's claim CAS was still to run on it. Fixed (`drained` after the loop; `jobs::wait()` keeps its Waiter in scope through the spin); the model gained the `drained` gate and a seeded `CRD_MODEL_EARLY_DRAINED` variant. Recorded verdicts (RC11, no unroll; one slot, one Waiter, four threads): repaired verifies, 6 complete + 2 blocked executions; broken handshake fails on the generation assertion; early drained fails on freed memory. (2026-10-11, found by the DIAG.10c workloads batch: the schedcheck driver-replay case failed 3 of 20 runs under linux-clang-tsan, and 7 of 40 on the previous scheduler, because a peer worker could finish the child before the root waited, so the root never parked and no scripted point occurred; in both park-point cases the child now sleeps 20 ms first; [session](sessions/2026-10-11-diag-10c-workloads.md).) Remaining (hosted): the first complete run of the GenMC step; [session](sessions/2026-10-10-diag-4b-genmc.md). **Done (2026-10-12):** the first complete run of the GenMC step, on `linux-clang-tsan` in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88`, built the pinned GenMC 0.19.0 against LLVM 20.1.2 and gave the three recorded verdicts: repaired verifies (6 complete and 2 blocked executions), the broken handshake fails on the generation assertion and the early-drained variant fails on freed memory. | 072 | <a id="slice-diag.4c"></a>**DIAG.4c** | Done | Hang/wait graphs, parked-fiber evidence, progress-sensitive watchdogs, starvation and real-time violation diagnostics; DG07. Landed on win-debug + real win-asan (29676 assertions / 171 cases): the full acceptance matrix of distinct reports — seeded **deadlock** (`HangKind::WaitCycle` from a Warshall closure over the wait graph's own→waiting-on edges, proven end-to-end by the `deadlock` subprocess specimen), **livelock** (opt-in per-task progress epochs; a mutual A/B livelock is reported for both tasks while the hang handler stays silent), **priority starvation** (a per-lane report off the injection queues' backlog/monotonic-pops; strict High→Normal→Low drain with no aging), **pool exhaustion** (a counted, always-on `CRD_FATAL` at the callers — the real Release bug was a null-counter deref / silently dropped job where `CRD_ASSERT_MSG` compiled out — proven by two subprocess specimens) and **forbidden blocking/allocation** (portable `RtScope` sentinels: the alloc check at the single `MemoryStats::on_allocate` seam covering `allocate`+`try_allocate`, the block check atop `jobs::wait()`; `CRD_ENABLE_ASSERTS`-gated). The reports are distinct (an RT violation fires only the RT handler with all four others installed; livelock is silent to the hang detector). No false deadlock claim on the four non-fault shapes: long progressing work reads Progressing; a badly overrun watchdog window is `Paused` (re-baselined, and the livelock check is skipped for it) covering debugger pause + suspend/resume; clock changes are excluded by construction (every interval is a `steady_clock` delta). Unresponsive processes get a bounded cooperative worker snapshot with an honest-incomplete result that still names the stuck task. Deferred by design (named in the session doc): wall-time deadline records, general lock/mutex-owner diagnostics and actual running-thread stack capture (all DIAG.5a/5b), the optional external observer, RTSan, and TSan qualification of the new plain snapshot reads (rides DIAG.1b). | 073 | <a id="slice-diag.5a"></a>**DIAG.5a** | Done | Reliable Windows crash capture, actual dump outcomes, stack exhaustion and collision-safe paths; DG09. Scoped by [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md) §112–120 to the **in-tree hardened `MiniDumpWriteDump` path** — no external/paid/newly-installed collector (that stays a separate future user decision). Landed on win-debug + real win-asan: a **checked, idempotent `install()`** (Ok / OkReinstalled / OutputDirUnusable; a latent double-install was fixed); **honest write results** (Ok only on a complete, flushed file — the success line is emitted solely under `wr == Ok`); **collision-safe long-path filenames** (`CreateFileW` + a `CREATE_NEW` attempt loop, `\\?\`-resolved, per-pid/tick/serial); an **off-faulting-stack dump** on a dedicated pre-created handler thread, so a stack overflow still dumps (main-thread + unguarded-worker + guarded-worker overflow specimens all capture `0xC00000FD`); a **single-shot gate** writing exactly one dump under concurrent faults (in-process two-thread Ok+Suppressed, plus a `concurrent` subprocess specimen); and a **lock-free `WriteFile` fatal path** that removes a 30 s hang when the faulter holds the CRT stderr lock (`logger_lock` specimen). The written dump is proven **readable and correctly identified** by reading it back with `read_dump_stream`: the `ExceptionStream` carries the fault code + faulting tid and the `ModuleListStream` names the crashing binary with an RSDS CV GUID/age — the "missing symbols" acceptance answered without symbolized frames (those are 5d). Failure modes are honest: injected `Open`/`Dump`/`Flush` seams each report their exact step and leave no partial (fatal-path `DumpFailed` proven via a handler-dropped marker); a `denied` output path is `OpenFailed`; every specimen is verified from OUTSIDE by the DIAG.0 harness to actually terminate with its retained NTSTATUS reason. Also delivered: a hang-triggered live dump carrying an evidence user stream (jobs `[hang-dump]`). Named limitations (per ADR): crash-while-**allocating** self-deadlocks DbgHelp on the heap lock (bounded by the 30 s `HandlerTimeout` fallback, then honest termination) and transfers to the future external collector; fiber/CET faults bypass `SetUnhandledExceptionFilter` (a VEH is needed — future work); under ASan the sanitizer's VEH owns access violations, so crd capture is validated on win-debug (asserted per mode, never skipped). Verified: `crd-core-tests` `[crash]`+`[crash-capture]` win-debug 24 cases / 121 assertions and win-asan 24 / 101; full core suite 26 / 133; both validators PASS. **2026-10-09 CI read:** The Windows `[crash]` and `[crash-capture]` cases are green on every Windows lane in complete runs [37852297628](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37852297628) (push) and [37915366121](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37915366121) (nightly) at `6bd1ba59`. Scoped by [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md) §112–120 to the **in-tree hardened `sigaction` record path** — no external/paid/newly-installed collector (a separate future user decision), so `capture_dump`/`read_dump_stream` are `Unsupported`/`0` on Linux **by design** (the OS core is the heavy artifact; crd adds the async-signal-safe record + honest termination; symbolization/backtraces are DIAG.5d, offline from the core — the handler never calls `backtrace`). Landed on `linux-gcc-debug`: a **per-thread alternate signal stack** (`guard_current_thread_stack` = `sigaltstack` of a 64 KiB `thread_local`; `install()` guards the main thread and `WorkerPool::worker_loop` guards every job worker), so a stack-exhaustion fault on a worker/fiber stack is captured — `overflow_thread` unguarded → 0 records (honest negative) vs `overflow_thread_guarded`/`fiber_overflow_worker` → 1 with `tid≠pid` + `SEGV_ACCERR`; an **async-signal-safe record** written with `write(2)` / collision-safe `O_EXCL` open / `close` only (no formatting, malloc or general locks — a `logger_lock` fault holding the stdio lock still records) carrying the signal, `si_code`/`si_addr`, original registers and pid/tid + the readlink'd exe, read back from disk (`av` → `addr 0x0`, register dump + `cr2` present, exe names the specimen); a **single-shot gate** (four fault signals masked + a lock-free winner/loser pair, no `SA_RESETHAND`) writing exactly one record under concurrent faults and bounding recursive faults (a `hook_fault` whose report hook itself faults still yields one record, no hang); **signal chaining** (record, restore the previous disposition, re-raise — a `chain` prior handler observed running via its marker); honest **install-failure** (`OutputDirUnusable` on a non-dir path) and **normal uninstall** (previous `sigaction` restored, `sigaltstack` `SS_DISABLE`); and process-local **core policy** (`crd_diag_harden` `setrlimit(RLIMIT_CORE,0)`, no global sysctl/security change). Measured platform differences from 5a, asserted explicitly: a `fiber` null-deref IS captured on Linux (pumped onto the guarded main thread; Windows needs a VEH → 0) and `__builtin_trap`→SIGILL IS captured (exit 132; Windows `__fastfail` bypasses SEH → 0). Uncatchable termination is honest: `SIGKILL` runs no handler, writes no record, and a pre-existing record is retained (exit 137). Named future work (tracked in the Session doc): `abort`/`fpe` fault modes and `inject_dump_fail` on Linux; ASan-build records are uncharacterized (no linux-asan tree — the ASan branch asserts only `exit≠0`, never skip-as-pass). | 075 | <a id="slice-diag.5c"></a>**DIAG.5c** | Done | Headless/fatal assertion policy and bounded reentrant logging/flush/shutdown; DG10. Landed (b)–(f), each acceptance clause declared-with-evidence (see the Session's clause table). **(b) headless assert default:** `set_assert_headless` → prompt `abort()` with stderr evidence instead of a modal `MessageBoxA`; on Linux `abort`→SIGABRT→the 5b emergency recorder (catastrophic route); the interactive break stays explicit (opt-in, default path unchanged; `assert_default` specimen proves no hang + declared exit, win-debug + Linux). **(c) bounded assert-path flush:** additive `flush_for(assert_flush_timeout_ms=2000)` (`wait_for` drain + `try_lock` sinks + worker self-detect) with `flush_timeout_count()` + stderr evidence, `flush()` unchanged; assert before-init/after-shutdown bypass the logger; a shutdown-race async push is a counted drop. **(e) logger failure policy:** a throwing sink is a per-sink counted failure (`sink_failure_count()`), never `std::terminate` on the noexcept worker nor a lost record for the other sinks; queue pressure = newest-dropped (`dropped_count()`), Critical retained via sync bypass; sink lifetime = logger lifetime; missing console fails silently. **(f) labels + audit:** `RingBufferSink` labeled NOT crash-safe (allocates per record; structural proof — no crd crash/signal handler logs, crash specimens link no crd-log); the assert-ignore table is now a bounded FIFO ring (`ignore_assert_site` + `assert_ignore_eviction_count`, one stderr line on first eviction) instead of silent-when-full; shipping input-rejection audit **clean** — the toml parser rejects malformed input via runtime `parse_error` (proven on win-shipping, asserts off), consumer asserts (config/profile/perf) are preconditions/writer-invariants. Lanes: win-debug + **win-shipping** (`/W4 /WX`, incl. `[ignore]` + toml `[toml]` asserts-off) + `linux-gcc-debug` `-Werror`; (b) regression-checked on win-asan `[crash-capture]` 11/25 (the `[crash]` cases 5a already counts are unchanged); **no sanitizer lane by design** (5c instruments no allocator/UB — per (a)'s regime). | 076 | <a id="slice-diag.5d"></a>**DIAG.5d** | Done | Versioned bounded crash/failure bundles, exact symbol identities, unloaded generations and adversarial import; DG14. Landed across (b)–(f) in `crd-perf` (`crd::` containers only): a **`cerid-diagnostics/1` bundle** — `BundleWriter`/`read_bundle` with a crc'd 40-byte header + per-section tag/flags/length/crc, explicit **absent** sections, honest **truncation** markers and **partial-section recovery** (`RecoveredTruncated`); **atomic publish** (`write_bundle_atomic`: tmp → flush-to-stable → rename, no leftover `.tmp`) and **retention** (`enforce_bundle_retention`, oldest-first eviction; ADR ≤256 MiB / ≤10 bundles). **Exact symbol identity**: a crd-native minidump byte-parser reads **every** module's RSDS **GUID/age + PDB name** and the **UnloadedModuleListStream** generation (a one-flag `MiniDumpWithUnloadedModules` add proved on a **real two-generation winhttp dump**); `identity_matches` **rejects wrong** GUID/age/build-id instead of guessing; `debug_file_path` builds the **symbol-store** (PDB by GUID/age) and Linux **`.build-id/<nn>/<rest>.debug`** conventions; **POSIX ELF build-id** captured at `install()` (async-signal-safe copy). **Trustworthy import**: `import_bundle` is a bounded, non-executing reader (borrows views, decodes POD, `noexcept`) that rejects oversized / too-many-sections (declared count peeked **before** the walk) / schema-mismatch / unsupported-version, flags oversized sections, refuses compressed payloads (v1 decompression-bomb defence), detects duplicate tags and **counts path-traversal** names; a **Manifest** (tag 1) that is redaction-honest **by design** (ID-8: no field/bit/API can declare the raw dump redacted; unknown flags reported, never interpreted); a **golden v1** byte-frozen migration guard. **Adversarial corpus + fuzz** (e2): a 10-seed committed corpus (truncations, traversal, oversized-length, decompression-bomb, wrong-schema, hostile `section_count`) replayed by `crd-fuzz-perf-bundle-corpus` on **every lane** and fuzzed under `CRD_ENABLE_FUZZER` (**libFuzzer 1,659,856 execs, 0 crashes** on linux-clang-fuzz). **Fresh-machine symbolization proven end-to-end** on a real dump (f): specimen → dump → `build_symbol_index_from_minidump` → bundle → `import_bundle` → every identity-bearing module resolves to a safe non-empty `debug_file_path` (a `None`-identity module resolves to `""` — refuse, don't guess), an unloaded generation survives, `age+1` is rejected. The frame→line **symbolizer is the offline consumer by design** (5b's rule); the **CLI executable** is routed to **DIAG.6c** (the bounded non-executing *library* is here); **dSYM/WASM** identities and an **N-1 reader** are named future work; **Linux unloaded-module attribution** is a named limitation (no OS-native list). | 078 | <a id="slice-diag.6b"></a>**DIAG.6b** | Done | Production CPU/job/GPU/I/O correlation, real frame-graph timestamp bridge and explicit clock uncertainty; DG11. Landed (a)–(k) in `crd::` containers. **(b) correlation wire format:** a sparse, appended CPROF `CorrelationRecord` side table (queue/device/clock-domain/uncertainty/pass/resource per sample), signalled by a flags bit — old readers ignore it, no `Sample` size bump. **(c) tracks:** per-(device,queue) tracks `"gpu d<dev> q<queue>"` (bounded `kMaxGpuTracks=16`, over-cap folds to an overflow track), `GpuSampleKind` Execution→Gpu / Transfer→Io / QueueWait→Wait. **(d) calibration + uncertainty:** `GpuClockCalibration` per device; calibrated → CPU timeline + a real bound, uncalibrated → raw GPU-tick domain with a sentinel uncertainty (never 0), counted. **(e) lifecycle:** `frame_mark` drives end_frame→resolve→begin_frame; a backend-neutral queue-id contract. **(f) resolve robustness:** narrow-counter single-wrap repair vs invalid-drop (both counted) + six backend-reported counters (unavailable / slot-reuse / device-loss / pending-dropped) — nothing fabricated to keep a count at zero. **(i) interface + bridge:** appended `pass_gpu_ticks`/`gpu_timestamp_period_ns`/`gpu_timestamp_valid_bits`/`pass_kind`/`pass_queue` to `IFrameGraph`; a new leaf module `crd-perf-gpu-bridge` (`FrameGraphGpuBackend`) places passes that expose ticks and counts the rest unavailable — never fabricates a begin from `pass_gpu_ms` (a duration). **(i2):** both raster backends retain the raw tick pairs and override the getters (DX12 period 1e9/freq, valid_bits 64; Vulkan cached graphics-family `timestampValidBits`, actual on_async queue, async passes unavailable — their stamps are graphics-timeline). **(j) oracle:** placed total (span, gaps included) vs the graph's own `gpu_ms_total` — match/mismatch/incomparable, derived tolerance, duration-vs-duration in one domain, magnitude-only on mismatch. **(k) real end-to-end:** a real DX12/Vulkan frame graph through the real bridge places both passes with linked `pass_id` (**DX12 55/1 + Vulkan 53/1 on real hardware**); every acceptance clause has a written verdict (Session clause table) — Satisfied-real or Satisfied-crd-perf-with-named-follow-up (queue-wait / resource / device-loss *producers*, per-queue query pools for real async-queue placement, and a resolve-failure-vs-empty-frame signal are DIAG.7a-shaped provider work, not 6b gaps). Verified: bridge `FakeFrameGraph` 57/12 (win-debug + win-asan); full device suites re-run this tick **DX12 10225/177 + Vulkan 6638/272** (each +23 assertions from the e2e block, PerfGuard isolation observed); `crd-perf-tests` 1234/192; the device-test exes build clean under **win-debug, win-shipping `/W4 /WX`, and `win-clang-cl-shipping` `/W4 /WX` thin-LTO** (the last needed the 8 perf headers guarded behind `#if CRD_PERF_ENABLED` — unconditional they ICE'd `lld-link` in the perf-off LTO TU); `check_no_std_containers` PASS; both validators PASS. Zero format bytes changed. win-debug `crd-perf-tests` 1256/196; `[cprof-version]` 22/4 (win-debug + win-asan); win-shipping compiles clean; my TU compiles clean under clang-cl-shipping (a pre-existing `-Winfinite-recursion` /WX defect in the DIAG.5d `crash_capture_specimen.cpp` dependency blocks that lane's full build — flagged separately, not 6c). **(c) Perfetto export landed** (2026-09-16): `export_perfetto_json` renders a `CaptureView` as offline Chrome/Perfetto trace-events JSON (scopes→X on pid 0 in µs; uncalibrated GPU→pid 1+device with RAW ticks, never rescaled; counters→typed C; frames on a reserved tid; M/i metadata + loss; `metadata` declares exporter/cprof_version/schema per ADR ID-1); JSON chosen over protobuf (offline, no dependency); all numbers integer-formatted (tr-TR locale-safe); `to_json` helpers hoisted to shared `json_writer.hpp` (zero behavior change). Observed: external `python json.load` gate → 10 events, ph={C,M,X}, pids=[0,8], numeric ts; teeth-verified (pid-domain break fails the test). win-debug `crd-perf-tests` 1280/197; `[perfetto]` 24/1 (win-debug + win-asan); win-shipping full link clean; clang-cl `crd-perf` (#else) clean. **(d) export/import acceptance landed** (2026-09-16): a test asserts native (`CaptureView`) == external (exported JSON) to the nanosecond — exact `"ts"/"dur"/"name"` fragments built from native `begin_ns`/`end_ns` via an independent µs-spec formatter, nesting (`outer.begin≤hot≤outer.end`, depth+1), counter `value:42` at the right frame, raw path exact; plus a container-free structural-JSON validator; teeth-verified (exporter ns-not-µs fails the equality). External trace_processor engine absent here → env-gated with the exact query command (Perfetto `slice.ts`==native `begin_ns` in ns). win-debug 1295/198; `[acceptance]` 16/1 (win-debug + win-asan); win-shipping clean. **(e) export CLI landed** (2026-09-16): `tools/cprof-export` → `cprof_export <in.cprof> <out.json>` wraps the same export path (links `crd-perf` only, not `crd-perf-ui`); new `load_capture_from_file` primitive; refuses with exit 3 when profiling is compiled out (never a zero-byte file — observed under clang-cl-shipping, which builds the CLI since a tools/ target has no crash-specimen dep); byte-identity oracle: `cprof_export` on the test's `.cprof` produced a json byte-IDENTICAL to the in-process export (`cmp` IDENTICAL, `json.load` OK). Registered (root `crd_module` + systems-map). win-debug + win-shipping + clang-cl-shipping build the CLI clean. **(f) external CPU-sampling runbook landed (artifacts + observed denial)** (2026-09-16): a scope-free hotspot specimen `crd-diag-cpu-hotspot-specimen` (`crd_diag_unscoped_hotspot_burn`, builds on win-debug + clang-cl-shipping), a verdict-machine runbook `scripts/sample-cpu-wpr.py` (wpr→xperf `-symbols -a profile -detail`→grep; try/finally `wpr -cancel`; local `_NT_SYMBOL_PATH` only), and `scripts/test-sample-cpu-wpr.py` 6/6. Observed: `wpr -start CPU` is DENIED here (`0xc5585011`, needs SeSystemProfilePrivilege) → runbook reports PERMISSION_DENIED (exit 3), no orphan session — clause 3 (hotspot visible via sampling) is **pending an elevated run the user performs**. **(g) optimized/fiber unwind qualification landed** (2026-09-16): the in-process oracle `tests/foundation/jobs/test_diag_unwind_qualification.cpp` (`[jobs][diag][unwind]`) records each function's `_ReturnAddress()` down a six-deep `noinline` chain (a `volatile` sink after every call defeats tail-call elision) and requires all appear in the `RtlCaptureStackBackTrace`/`backtrace` capture in **strictly increasing frame order** — a symbol-free, exact unwind oracle that reads the same `.pdata`/`.xdata` tables WPA/xperf do. Optimized native frames unwind correctly on win-debug + **win-shipping (`/O2`, the point — win-debug keeps frame pointers)** + **win-clang-cl-shipping** (41/41 each); the test lives in `crd-jobs-tests`, so flip-blocker 1's `crash_capture_specimen` defect does NOT gate the LTO lane here. crd-jobs fibers driven raw via the public `fiber_init_stack`/`fiber_switch` unwind their own frames AND **truncate at the hand-rolled switch boundary** (9 vs the thread's 24 frames, bounded — no runaway past the trampoline): the qualified limitation the acceptance demands, documented not silently dropped. Teeth (observed on `/O2`): force-inlining one frame collapses it → the strict-order `REQUIRE` fails (`out of order at index 4 (prev 4)`, exit 42) → restored + rebuilt → 41/41. External half: `scripts/sample-cpu-wpr.py --stacks` records the `Profile` stackwalk and processes `-a stack` (both verified against the installed `xperf -help`) with a new `MISSING_STACKS` verdict distinct from `HOTSPOT_NOT_VISIBLE`; `scripts/test-sample-cpu-wpr.py` **9/9**; observed PERMISSION_DENIED (`0xc5585011`) here, no orphan. **Clause 2 is SATISFIED in-process** on the shipping + clang-cl lanes; the external `-a stack` confirmation is elevation-gated and rides blocker (2). **(h) rich counters "where supported" landed** (2026-09-16): `scripts/counters-capability.py` is a capability/verdict machine recording each counter class (context_switch / page_fault / cache_branch_pmu / lock_contention / cpu_numa_affinity) as AVAILABLE / MULTIPLEXING_REQUIRED / PERMISSION_DENIED / UNAVAILABLE(reason) / TOOL_MISSING — **never a silent zero**, exactly the design clause ("record permissions, multiplexing and missing PMU events rather than treating unavailable counters as zero"). Unprivileged enumeration (`xperf -pmcsources` / `-providers KF`, syntax checked vs the installed help) runs here: all five classes AVAILABLE, the PMU is real (16 cache/branch sources, **max 8 concurrent** — one of the 9 profile slots is the timer, observed). Elevation-gated `--capture` observed on this box → PERMISSION_DENIED (NT Kernel Logger `0x5` / PMC-config), `--pmu-count 12` → MULTIPLEXING_REQUIRED, no orphaned session. Two defects caught by live observation (not recall): the N−1 timer-slot reservation, and a counter-count limit first mis-reported as PERMISSION_DENIED — now classified MULTIPLEXING_REQUIRED. `scripts/test-counters-capability.py` 11/11; clause SATISFIED as worded, the elevated capture riding blocker (2). **(i) frame pacing / input-to-present landed** (2026-09-16): `engine/foundation/perf/include/crd/perf/frame_pacing.hpp` — a pure, bounded `FramePacingAnalyzer` (fixed-bucket histograms, `crd::` only, integer ns) with THREE deliberately separate axes: pacing (frame-to-frame interval distribution — mean/p99/max/stutter-count), work (per-frame duration `end−begin`), and input-to-present latency (`present−input`). All computed from the **existing** `FrameRecord::frame_begin_ns`/`frame_end_ns`, so NO on-disk field is added and NO CPROF version bump is forced (the on-disk present/input timestamps are a named v2 follow-up, not smuggled into (i)); `feed_frames()` consumes a `CaptureView::frame_records()` span. `tests/foundation/perf/test_diag_frame_pacing.cpp` (`[perf][diag][pacing]`, 22 assertions / 4 cases) proves the SEPARATION the clause demands: equal throughput → different pacing tail (p99 ≥5 ms higher, stutter 50 vs 0); equal pacing → different work (4 vs 12 ms); input-to-present (48 ms, 3-frame pipeline) ≠ frame duration (16 ms). Teeth: making the histogram `bucket_of` ignore its value breaks the binning → cases 1 (pacing tail) & 3 (latency percentile) fail (exit 42), cases 2 & 4 pass — proving the histogram distinguishes the distributions; restored + rebuilt. Lanes: win-debug + win-asan + win-shipping (`/O2`) 22/22; the clang-cl TU compiles clean (`/W4 /WX -flto=thin`, object produced), the exe link riding blocker 1. Clause SATISFIED with no on-disk/version change. **(j) platform-debugger recipes landed** (2026-09-16): `scripts/debugger-recipes.py` carries recipes (in its header, script-is-runbook like (f)/(h)) mapping the clause's three targets to a debugger command AND an in-engine equivalent — uninstrumented work → WinDbg `!runaway` / lldb `break;run;bt` + the (f) WPR sampler; blocked time → `!analyze -v -hang` / `thread apply all bt` + the DIAG.4c hang watchdog/wait-graph, with the honest (g) cross-ref that a parked crd fiber sits on NO OS-thread stack (register-swap `fiber_switch`) so a debugger shows idle workers not the blocked fibers; memory pressure → `!address`/`!heap` / lldb `memory region` + the 3c DiagnosticAllocator + (h) `page_fault` — plus an availability verdict machine. Observed on this box (looked, not assumed): **lldb present but UNRUNNABLE** (`0xC0000135` STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND — `liblldb.dll` imports `python310.dll`, absent; box has Python 3.14), **cdb/windbg/gdb absent** → no runnable external interactive debugger, recorded first-class (never a faked session), cross-referenced to the engine's own crash/hang/allocator/counter diagnostics. `scripts/test-debugger-recipes.py` 6/6. Clause SATISFIED (recipes + in-engine equivalents; availability honest). **6c is now (a)–(j) COMPLETE — no agent-doable 6c work remains; it stays Open only on the two user-gated blockers below.** **Flip-blocking (BOTH must clear):** (1) the `crash_capture_specimen.cpp` clang-cl `-Winfinite-recursion` fix (`task_f28fe517`) so `crd-perf-tests` builds under clang-cl-shipping; (2) an elevated `sample-cpu-wpr.py` run confirming HOTSPOT_VISIBLE for the flat profile (clause 3) and `--stacks` call stacks (clause 2 external half). **User decision (2026-10-05):** the user performs one elevated `sample-cpu-wpr.py --stacks` run (blocker 2); blocker 1 was committed in 660a0857 and awaits CI confirmation; no hosted sampling lane. **Both blockers cleared (2026-10-07).** (1) The clang-cl `-Winfinite-recursion` fix is confirmed by the green complete-tier runs (nightly 37445373995, 37456628683, 37502078114): `win-clang-cl-shipping` builds `crd-perf-tests`. (2) The user's elevated WPR capture (process 26256, 18,194 samples) shows the unscoped hotspot through sampling: `HOTSPOT_VISIBLE` for the flat profile (clause 3) and for walked call stacks `__scrt_common_main → invoke_main → main → crd_diag_unscoped_hotspot_burn`, 4,093 inclusive and 2,635 exclusive samples (clause 2, external half). The capture also exposed a runbook defect, now fixed: `--stacks` exported with `xperf -a stack` and no activity, which xperf rejects (exit 5, empty output), so the run printed a failed export. The export now uses `-butterfly -process <specimen>`, the frame check accepts `module ! symbol`, and `--from-etl` re-analyses a recorded trace without elevation; both verdicts were reproduced from the user's trace that way. Found — validation capture ALREADY exists on BOTH backends (DX12 `ID3D12InfoQueue1`/`EnableDebugLayer`; Vulkan `crd::gpu::ValidationCapture` on a `VK_EXT_debug_utils` messenger), public headers already vendor-clean (`Dx12ValidationSeverity` / `ValidationSeverity`, crd:: containers — no `D3D12_*`/`Vk*`); DX12's clean-run predicate already treats **dropped messages as non-clean**; Vulkan images get SITE+SIZE debug-utils labels (8 call sites). Missing/parallel — the two backends' vocabularies are NOT unified (ADR's "one collector, no parallel" → unify into a common `crd::gpu` contract, which 7b/7c then extend); passes carry only STRING names (`add_pass(const char*)`), and there is NO stable Cerid resource/program/pass **ID + generation** attached to native object names/reports (only human site labels); no unified per-mode (core/sync/GPU-assisted) activation + unsupported-reason surface. Real GPU here → the acceptance (intentional lifetime/state/descriptor hazard → a **correlated** error on each claimed route; valid consumers stay clean; dropped ≠ clean) is a **device test observed on this box, both APIs**. Choice point recorded: identity "stable across" = reload/recreation (ID stable, generation bumps), mirroring the 3e handle/generation + 5d identity precedent — confirm vs DG12. **(b) common validation vocabulary landed** (2026-09-16): a backend-agnostic `crd::gpu` contract `engine/gpu/gpu-context/include/crd/gpu/validation.hpp` (`ValidationSeverity` + `ValidationReport{info,warning,error,dropped,truncated,instrumentation_failures; clean()}`) both captures project onto — `clean()` encodes the acceptance rule "dropped validation messages are not a clean run". Vulkan drops its local `ValidationSeverity` (now the common one — one definition, no ODR clash, every consumer keeps the `crd::gpu::ValidationSeverity` name) and **exposes the previously-unreachable `records_dropped`** via new `dropped_count()`/`report()` — closing a real gap: every Vulkan clean-run gate asserts only `error_count()==0 && warning_count()==0` (~40 sites, none bounds dropped/`messages().size()`), so a run that dropped messages would read as clean (structural — the gates cannot see drops; not a claim a hidden error was observed). DX12 keeps its rich native types (lifecycle 7b needs) + gains inline `to_common()` mappers (additive, no consumer breakage); the report mapper **folds instrument-failure lifecycle** — not-`Ready` / `device_creation_failures` / `execution_failures` → common `instrumentation_failures` — so a message-silent but never-started DX12 report maps to NON-clean (the registered-default-empty-reads-as-clean scar). Oracle `tests/gpu/gpu-context/test_diag_validation_vocab.cpp` (`[gpu][diag][validation][vocab]`, 10 assertions, device-free) + DX12 projection `[dx12][validation][vocab]` (13 assertions) in `test_dx12_validation.cpp`; teeth (win-debug): removing the `dropped==0` term fails the gpu-context dropped case (1/2) → 10/10; dropping the DX12 readiness/device/execution fold makes the not-`Ready` case read as clean → fails → restored + rebuilt → 13/13. Lanes: the 3 GPU backend libs build on win-debug + win-shipping (`/O2 /WX`) + win-clang-cl-shipping (`-flto=thin`); `[vocab]` 10/10 on all three (gpu-context) + 13/13 (dx12 win-debug, TU links under shipping + clang-cl). Live per-backend dropped-injection on a real device is (g)'s acceptance specimen (`vkSubmitDebugUtilsMessageEXT` is in this box's SDK → feasible), not smuggled into (b). **(c) stable object identity value type landed** (2026-09-16): `engine/gpu/gpu-context/include/crd/gpu/object_identity.hpp` (header-only, no vendor types) defines `ObjectKind{Resource,Program,Pass}` + `struct ObjectIdentity{kind; u32 index; u32 generation; valid(); ==}` whose (index, generation) layout **mirrors `crd::containers::SlotMap::Handle` verbatim** (index 0xFFFFFFFF = null, generation 0 reserved, live generations start at 1 and bump on recreation — the alias-proof-on-reuse model the design demands: "IDs cannot silently alias when a GPU object is reused"). Name contract: `encode()` writes fixed-width ASCII lowercase hex `crd:<kind>:<8hex idx>:g<8hex gen>` (hand-formatted, locale-independent; REFUSES an invalid identity, returns 0), `parse()` scans for the token INSIDE a longer string (a debug-layer message quotes the name in prose) and accepts only a valid() token. Choice point resolved: the requirement rides the §7a clause + ADR schema clause 7 / record-schema (design L58-65, "IDs cannot silently alias … on reuse") = stable across reload/recreation, generation bumps (the DG tags live in the research doc; DG12 "GPU fault depth" is 7b/7c's, not a 7a identity driver — census corrects the (a) attribution). Oracle `tests/gpu/gpu-context/test_diag_object_identity.cpp` (`[gpu][diag][identity]`, device-free, 86 assertions/8 cases): validity matches SlotMap, round-trip over every kind×index×generation boundary, exact hex output, encode-refuses-invalid (canary), parse-embedded + past-a-malformed-`crd:`, rejects wrong-prefix/unknown-kind/non-hex/missing-`:g`/truncated/gen-0, too-small-buffer refuses with a one-past-end canary intact. Error-severity program correlation on DX12 is a Core mechanism limit (7b GBV/DRED); whether warning severity satisfies the clause is part of the open user decision. **User decision (2026-10-05):** DIAG.7b(g) is the error-severity DX12 program proof. The lifetime class is built (retired-handle provenance with a use-after-destroy specimen on both APIs). The DX12 pass label uses the core BeginEvent/EndEvent marker, measured first and correlated through DRED contexts. No silent waivers. **Lifetime class proven** (2026-10-05) on both APIs: a destroyed or in-flight-released Cerid-named buffer yields an error correlated to its retired identity. On Vulkan it is the record-time error for an invalidated secondary command buffer (never submitted); on DX12 it is 921 OBJECT_DELETED_WHILE_STILL_IN_USE, with the held copy discarded by RemoveDevice. Controls are clean and the teeth hold. The premise that names are lost was measured false (VVL keeps them), so no handle table was built. **DX12 pass label emitted** (core BeginEvent/EndEvent carrying the pass identity, no PIX, wired around every frame-graph pass). The debug layer offers no local oracle (measured), so the pass-to-fault proof rides DRED contexts on the WARP real fault (7b h1). **DX12 pass route (2026-10-07):** the removal report now resolves the pass a stopped command list was inside from DRED's breadcrumb contexts (the innermost open marker carrying a Pass identity), proven on constructed lists with teeth. WARP produces no real fault (DIAG.7b(h1), measured), so the device proof moves to the user-run hardware fault DIAG.7b(h2), which asserts the faulting pass. **Remaining (user-only):** that (h2) run. All other routes are proven locally. **Hosted CI must show** the 7a cases green on the Windows lanes and on lavapipe. **DX12 pass-to-fault (2026-10-07):** the hardware proof is an explicitly unqualified route by user decision, because the user's GPU produced no real fault (DIAG.7b(h2), two runs). The pass resolver stays proven on constructed lists with teeth and on the WARP child. With every other route proven locally and not yet published, the row waits only for its first hosted run. **2026-10-09 CI read:** the hosted clause is shown. The four DX12 7a cases pass with assertions on every Windows lane (WARP, `Microsoft Basic Render Driver`), and the six Vulkan cases pass with assertions on lavapipe on every Linux lane, in complete runs [37852297628](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37852297628) (push) and [37915366121](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37915366121) (nightly) at `6bd1ba59`. The hosted Windows runners have no Vulkan device: 7a(f) reports skipped there and the other five Vulkan cases return early with a warning and no assertions, so lavapipe is their only hosted evidence. Nothing local or user-side remains; the row waits on [DIAG.4b](#slice-diag.4b) and [DIAG.6c](#slice-diag.6c) in table order; [audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md#rows-closed-on-6bd1ba59-2026-10-09). **Done (2026-10-12):** the four DX12 7a cases pass with assertions on every Windows lane (WARP), and the eight 7a cases pass on every Linux lane, the six Vulkan ones on lavapipe (with assertions in all four Linux evidence bundles read), in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88`.
