simulated RemoveDevice: DRED on -> Ok, off -> NotAvailable, live -> NotAvailable. **(d) landed** (2026-10-04): stop_failed_device records every stopped device (Observed vs EngineForced) with its DRED report in a process-lifetime store; dx12_wait timeout test proves both origins. **(e) landed** (2026-10-04): breadcrumb list/queue and page-fault allocation names map to Cerid identities (retired kept); forced RemoveDevice measured to yield no breadcrumb nodes, so content needs (h); mapping proven on constructed DRED lists. **(f) landed** (2026-10-05): each recorded removal is also a DeviceRemoved live dump (gpu_*.dmp) with a versioned CRDR evidence bundle; dx12_read_removal_bundle reads it back or refuses NoStream/BadMagic/BadVersion/BadSize. **(g) landed** (2026-10-05): an uninitialized-descriptor compute dispatch on a Cerid-named pipeline reports DESCRIPTOR_UNINITIALIZED as an error naming its Program identity only under GBV (guarded, production submit/wait, device live); the core layer alone is silent; fixed a pre-existing GBV leak into no-request scopes (it faulted a later frame-graph test). Next (h) hardware-gated real fault. **User decision (2026-10-05):** (h) splits into (h1), a real page fault on WARP in a child process (labelled software evidence), and (h2), one opt-in hardware page-fault run started by the user. **(h1) measured (2026-10-07): WARP gives no real fault.** It discards stores to an unmapped GPU address (a released buffer, 1 GiB past a live one, a wild address) with the device live, and has no watchdog for a never-ending shader (55 s); the engine's forced removal then leaves DRED's breadcrumb list empty. The bounded child (`test_dx12_device_fault.cpp`) stays as the CI-capable WARP run of the whole path, labelled software evidence: it must end "completed" with no bundle, or "recorded" with the full bundle checked. The removal bundle is version 2: breadcrumb nodes keep their op history, context strings and the in-flight pass (teeth on the resolver and on the child); version 3 since DIAG.9b added the `Simulated` origin ([device loss](sessions/2026-10-10-diag-9b-device-loss.md)). Lanes: win-debug (whole DX12 exe, 203 cases), win-shipping, win-clang-cl-shipping and win-asan. **Remaining (user-only): (h2)**, the real fault. On an idle machine, `set CRD_DX12_HARDWARE_FAULT_OPT_IN=1`, then `build\win-debug\tests\gpu-context-dx12\crd-gpu-context-dx12-tests.exe "[.dx12-hardware-device-fault]" -s`. It must show an observed removal with DRED state PageFault or Fault, the in-flight pass resolved to the faulting pass, and the released buffer among the recently freed allocations. **Hosted CI must show** `[dred]` green on the Windows lanes, with the (h1) WARP case passing. **User run and decision (2026-10-07): (h2) is an explicitly unqualified route.** On the user's RTX 4070 Ti SUPER the opt-in child completed both times (exit 30): first with the buffer released just before submission, then with the release flushed on the queue and a 500 ms pause. The device stayed live, and DRED recorded nothing. The driver does not turn a GPU use-after-free into a page fault, so a real DX12 fault bundle on hardware is listed as unproven, never claimed. The flush-and-pause hazard and the opt-in case stay. With (a)–(h1) done locally and not yet published, the row waits only for its first hosted run. **Done (2026-10-12):** the seven `[dred]` cases are green on every Windows lane in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88`, with both (h1) cases passing: the WARP child completed with no device fault and no bundle (the declared software outcome), and the pass resolver passed with 34 assertions. No user item remains: (h2) is an explicitly unqualified route by the 2026-10-07 user decision; [audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md#rows-closed-on-7a958b88-2026-10-12). | [DIAG.7a](#slice-diag.7a) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-7b); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Census](sessions/2026-10-04-diag-7b-census.md); [Decisions 2026-10-05](sessions/2026-10-05-user-decisions.md); [(h1) WARP measurement](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7b-h1-warp-fault-and-pass-resolution.md); [User items 2026-10-07](sessions/2026-10-07-user-items-wpr-and-vulkan-loss.md); [Complete run 2026-10-12](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326); [Needs CI audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md) |
| 082 | <a id="slice-diag.7c"></a>**DIAG.7c** | Done | Vulkan synchronization/GPU-assisted validation and supported device-fault evidence on Windows/Linux; DG12. **(a) census landed** (2026-10-05): DIAG.7a already qualifies mode activation, a core hazard, a sync missing-barrier WAW and a GPU-assisted out-of-bounds report after the fence. Still missing: the layer-absent path (it currently invalidates the context), layer/SDK version evidence, the instrumentation descriptor-slot fallback, bounded submit/wait with VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST retention, VK_EXT_device_fault collection (the workstation's RTX 4070 Ti SUPER advertises it), stale-resource cases and a loss bundle. Plan (b)-(h); a real loss stays a hardware gate, and hosted lanes have no Vulkan device. **(b) landed** (2026-10-05): the layer is enumerated before it is requested, and `validation_layer()` records presence and spec/implementation versions (VVL 1.4.341 here). With the layer disabled through the loader filter, a valid context reports LayerAbsent for every requested mode (previously the factory returned null). **(c) landed** (2026-10-05): bounded `vk_submit` and `vk_wait` classify each failure (Observed/TimedOut/Failed/Simulated) and keep the first one per device through later failures. A lost device is not called again, the last-known failure outlives the device, and a reused handle starts clean. The compute context latches invalid on failure. An injected loss and a contained timed-out wait are proven on hardware. The raster (present, upload batches, one-shots, frame graph), RT and DGC paths now submit and wait through the seam too, with `vk_queue_wait_idle`/`vk_device_wait_idle` for the unboundable idle waits; a fence or semaphore is waited on only when its submit succeeded, removing a latent infinite wait after a failed submit. **(d) landed** (2026-10-05): `VK_EXT_device_fault` is enabled when advertised (the RTX 4070 Ti SUPER; lavapipe lacks it), and the seam reads `vkGetDeviceFaultInfoEXT` once when a device is first recorded lost, into a fixed report (description, up to 8 address and 8 vendor infos, counts, the query result) kept as last-known state after the device is destroyed. On an injected loss the RTX driver answers `VK_ERROR_UNKNOWN` (the device is not really lost), which is recorded; a real loss stays the hardware gate. **User decision (2026-10-05):** a real loss follows 7b's (h1)/(h2) split; lavapipe on the hosted Linux lanes makes the Vulkan cases CI-run. **First lavapipe run** (2026-10-05) found four problems, all fixed and reproduced on the WSL reference host. (1) The 30 s bound abandoned in-flight work, so slow sorts segfaulted; `vk_wait_complete` now reports a slow completion and waits it out, proven with a timeline-semaphore gate. (2) The GLSL task shader did not pass a payload to a payload-declaring mesh (VUID-RuntimeSpirv-MeshEXT-10883). (3) The GPU-AV specimen executed its out-of-bounds store; GPU-AV now runs only in the layer's safe mode, and the specimen is memory-safe by construction. (4) The lifetime specimens executed against destroyed objects. B4-vis-2 was a real emitter defect: every compute dialect hoisted guarded buffer reads above their `if`, so empty pixels read far out of bounds; `KernelEmissionOrder` now keeps computed-address reads in order (five-dialect teeth). **(e) and (f) landed** (2026-10-07). (e): GPU-assisted instrumentation takes the device's last descriptor-set slot, and the layer does not lower the limit it reports. The context now reports the slot (`VulkanValidationLayer::instrumentation_set_slot`, `instruments(n)`), and the capture classifies the layer's GPU-AV setup warning as an instrumentation refusal, carried by the common `ValidationReport::instrumentation_failures`. On the RTX (limit 32), a 31-set layout runs the out-of-bounds specimen instrumented and correlated. A 32-set layout is refused at creation, and the same dispatch then reports nothing; shader objects behave the same. (f): a Cerid-named image cleared while still `UNDEFINED` is silent at record and yields an error correlated to its live identity by the fence wait, and the transitioned control is clean. The GPU-AV specimen asserts nothing before the wait. The destroyed-resource case is the DIAG.7a lifetime test. Teeth: classifier off, slot off by one, hazard leg transitioned. **(g) landed** (2026-10-07): the first time a device is recorded lost, after its fault report is read, the seam writes a pointer-free `VkLossRecord` (first failure, loss origin and operation, counts, adapter and driver, fault report) as a `DeviceRemoved` live dump with its own magic and version, once per device and never for a failure that is not a loss; `vk_read_loss_bundle` refuses foreign (DX12's included), other-version and other-size evidence. `lost()` now follows the first `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` even after an earlier timeout. On the RTX the injected loss round-trips (labelled Simulated, with the driver's fault-query result); on Linux the loss is kept in process and the write reports `Unsupported` (live dumps are Windows-only, DIAG.5a/5b). Teeth: write skipped, old `lost()` rule, reader version. **Remaining (user):** (h) per the 2026-10-05 split. Two loop steps on 2026-10-07 stopped before building the software leg, so (h) now waits for the user: decide whether and how the lavapipe child that measures a real `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` is built (h1), and run the opt-in hardware leg (h2) ([note](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7c-h-handed-to-user.md)). **Hosted run must show** the (e), (f), (g) and DIAG.7a(g-3) cases passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes, with (g) reporting `Unsupported`; the hosted Windows lanes have no Vulkan device. **User decision (2026-10-07): (h) is an explicitly unqualified route.** No software (lavapipe) or hardware real-device-loss test is built. The loss path stands on the injected-loss, fault-report and loss-bundle proofs (c)–(g); a real `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` on a physical GPU remains unproven and is listed as such, never claimed. The DX12 counterpart, DIAG.7b(h2), is also an explicitly unqualified route: the user's two hardware runs completed without a fault. With (a)–(g) done locally and not yet published, the row waits only for its first hosted run (below). **Done (2026-10-12):** the (e), (f), (g) and DIAG.7a(g-3) cases pass on lavapipe on every Linux lane, with assertions in all four Linux evidence bundles read in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88` (90, 33, 14 and 35 assertions on linux-gcc-debug), (g) checking the `Unsupported` write on Linux; all eight 7c cases are green on the Linux lanes and report skipped on the hosted Windows lanes, which have no Vulkan device. No user item remains: (h) is an explicitly unqualified route by the 2026-10-07 user decision; [audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md#rows-closed-on-7a958b88-2026-10-12). | [DIAG.7b](#slice-diag.7b) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-7c); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Census](sessions/2026-10-05-diag-7c-census.md); [Decisions 2026-10-05](sessions/2026-10-05-user-decisions.md) ; [(e)+(f)](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7c-descriptor-limit-and-stale-layout.md); [(g)](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7c-g-loss-bundle.md); [(h) to the user](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7c-h-handed-to-user.md); [User items 2026-10-07](sessions/2026-10-07-user-items-wpr-and-vulkan-loss.md); [Capability skips](sessions/2026-10-10-diag-7-vulkan-capability-skips.md); [Complete run 2026-10-12](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326); [Needs CI audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md) |
| 083 | <a id="slice-diag.8a"></a>**DIAG.8a** | Done | CHIR/CEIR/CKIR-compatible authored-source and lowering provenance through cook/optimization/reload; DG13. **CEIR core landed** (2026-10-07): measured first that the parser recorded no op location, `Operation::loc` is content (in `stable_hash`), passes dropped origins and plan errors were bare enums. Provenance is now a Context side table plus a skippable `ORIG` binary chunk, outside the content hash, CSE equality and the text (`crd/ceir/provenance.hpp`). `parse(ctx, text, file_id)` records each op's line:col and reports a parse error's line:col; CSE survivors and folds carry the union of the replaced ops' origins by stable id; the compiled plan owns per-instr sites, and `CompileResult::op`, `RunResult::fault` and the interpreter's `ExecResult::op` resolve to the authored line; unknown attribution is a typed gap. `test_provenance.cpp` (6 cases) runs text, CSE, serialize, fresh-Context load, compile and a BadForStep run to the authored `core.for` line, shows reformatting moves origins but no hash, and rejects a corrupt `ORIG`; teeth on CSE, `ORIG` and the fault latch. **Cook and reload landed** (2026-10-07): `cook_program_text(ctx, source, file, ...)` and `ReloadSet::add_source`/`reload_source(id, source, file)` cook under the named file, so the installed generation resolves runtime faults to `file:line:col`; a failed cook reports a POD `CookSite` (parse line:col, or the rejected op's authored origin, or a typed gap) copied out of the transient Context into `AddResult`/`ReloadResult`; the installed generation keeps its file through failed reloads, and a reformat-only `NoChange` reload moves its positions while keeping its handles. `test_reload_provenance.cpp` (3 cases) with teeth on file registration, the site fill, the copy-out and the refresh. **CHIR lowering landed** (2026-10-07): `lower_chir(m, ctx, file)` records a ChirNode origin (CHIR id plus line:col under the registered file, never the model's own file numbering) on every op it creates; the `core.state` cell names its StateDecl and the folded StateUpdate; graph-authored nodes keep their CHIR id with an explicit no-source-location gap, which `render_provenance` now lists. After CSE, serialization and a fresh-Context load, the committed program's outer-capture refusal (`CapturedValue`) and the interpreter's `UndefinedValue` name the authored `parallel_for` line; the self-contained variant compiles with every instr naming its node. `test_chir_provenance.cpp` (5 cases) with teeth on the fold, the file re-stamp and the render. **Host provider landed** (2026-10-07): measured first that the crd-jobs provider and the sequential reference blamed a parallel body, fold step or pooled launch body error on the owning `parallel_for`/`map_reduce`/`await` op while the plan named the inner instr. `Interpreter::failed_op()` now lets both blame the body op that raised the error (owner only when none), keeping first-in-index-order. `test_host_provenance.cpp` (4 cases) runs text, CSE, serialize and a fresh-Context load, then shows provider (job splits 1-8), reference and plan naming the same authored inner `core.for` line, index 0's loop B over program-first loop A, and the owner for its own fault; teeth on all four sites. **Entries, generations and CHIR reload landed** (2026-10-07): a lookup-failed `NoEntry` from the interpreter, the plan compiler and the crd-jobs provider carries an owned copy of the requested entry name (`entry`); `ReloadSet::locate` names the asset, the generation that ran a fault (matched by number: Current, Retiring or Gone), its content hash and the site rendered in that generation's own Context, so an old plan still running after a hot swap names generation 1 and its file while the new one names generation 2; a CHIR-lowered, cooked and installed program keeps its `ChirNode` origins (CHIR id, line:col, file) through a truncated blob, a contract change and an unparsable source, and a reformat-only CHIR reload moves them with the same ids. New cases in `test_provenance.cpp`, `test_host_provenance.cpp`, `test_reload_provenance.cpp` and `test_chir_reload_provenance.cpp` (2) with teeth on both entry copies, the install number, the generation match, the refresh gate and the refreshed space. **Intrinsic sites landed** (2026-10-07): `native_binding(ctx, op)` reads the ADR-0110 binding the registration already carries (`Intrinsic` with its declared provider, `NotIntrinsic`, or `Unregistered` when the Context cannot know, never reported as non-intrinsic), and `render_op_site` prints `<op> native <provider> at <file:line:col op#id>` for every reporter; the host scene resolver now blames every `UnresolvedSceneHandle` (unwired callback or 0 return) and `SceneChainMisuse` on the responsible resolve op (`SceneResolvedHandles::fault`). After text, CSE, serialize and a fresh-Context load, the reference `NoSemantics` and the plan's `UnsupportedOp` name `scene.resolve_material native host` at its authored line, and in a Context without the scene dialect the same op is `Unregistered`. New case in `test_provenance.cpp` and `tests/execution/ceir-gpu/test_scene_provenance.cpp` (2) with teeth on the intrinsic check, the refusal op and the misuse op. **GPU dispatch and CKIR record sites landed** (2026-10-07): with a `DispatchSites` table `execute_lowered` labels each recorded dispatch with a fresh Pass identity (`[<id>] compute.dispatch @<kernel>`, `ComputeRecorder::begin_label`/`end_label`, recorded on Vulkan) and the Vulkan capture keeps that label in `ValidationMessage::label` beside the object identity; a refused execute names the refused dispatch (`fault()`); a refused `.ckir` names its positional record (`CkirReadResult::pool`/`index`) and line:col. On the RTX 4070 Ti SUPER a Core `VUID-vkCmdDispatch-groupCountY-00387` raised by an authored over-limit grid (recorded, never submitted) maps through the label to the second `compute.dispatch` at its authored `file:line:col`, after text, CSE, serialize and load and again in a ReloadSet generation kept through a failed reload; measured that VVL 1.4.341 syncval reports no missing barrier between compute dispatches without `syncval_shader_accesses_heuristic`. `test_dispatch_provenance.cpp` (3), `test_dispatch_provenance_vulkan.cpp` (1) and a `test_ckir_asset.cpp` case, with teeth on the label, the capture's label field, the retire, the fault and the CKIR record. **CHIR graph-schema sites landed** (2026-10-07): `read_schema` returns `SchemaReadResult` (the `ChirParseResult` mold) naming the offending record's line and token column, the CHIR node it declares or names (with its stable id) and, for an orphaned layout row, the unresolved id; record fields must sit on the record's line, a second writer is blamed on the later edge, and whole-graph checks report in document order. On the committed `.chirgraph` with one authored mistake each (13 kinds), the named node's id leads to its line in the `.chir` text; teeth on the later-edge blame, the line-bound fields and the orphan line. **All local clauses are shown**; the row is not yet published, so it waits only for its first hosted run, which must show `crd-ceir-tests` (542 cases), `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (41), `crd-chir-tests` (29), `crd-ceir-host-tests` (29), `crd-ceir-gpu-tests` (137) and `crd-kir-tests` green on all six lanes with their `diag 8a` cases passing, and `crd-ceir-gpu-vulkan-tests`' `diag 8a` case passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes (it skips on the hosted Windows lanes, which have no Vulkan device). **Done (2026-10-12):** `crd-ceir-tests` (576 cases now), `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (115), `crd-chir-tests` (30), `crd-ceir-host-tests` (55), `crd-ceir-gpu-tests` (140) and `crd-kir-tests` (313) are green on win-debug, win-asan, win-shipping, linux-gcc-debug, linux-gcc-asan and linux-clang-tsan with their 32 `diag 8a` cases, and `crd-ceir-gpu-vulkan-tests`' `diag 8a` case passes with 87 assertions on lavapipe, in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88`. Later rows added the extra cases. On the hosted Windows lanes the Vulkan case returns with a warning and no assertions; [audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md#rows-closed-on-7a958b88-2026-10-12). | [DIAG.7c](#slice-diag.7c) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-8a); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [CEIR core](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-ceir-provenance.md); [cook/reload](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-cook-and-reload-provenance.md); [CHIR lowering](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-chir-lowering-provenance.md); [host provider](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-host-provider-provenance.md); [entries/generations/CHIR reload](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-entries-generations-and-chir-reload.md); [intrinsic sites](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-intrinsic-provider-sites.md); [GPU dispatch/CKIR sites](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-gpu-dispatch-and-ckir-sites.md); [CHIR graph sites](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-chir-graph-schema-sites.md); [Complete run 2026-10-12](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326); [Needs CI audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md) |
| 084 | <a id="slice-diag.8b"></a>**DIAG.8b** | Done | Safe-point inspection/stepping and typed value snapshots through existing production execution; DG13. **Plan and interpreter safe points landed** (2026-10-07): measured first that the interpreter's §112 step hook had no user and let a held op run after a cancel, the compiled plan had no cancel and no slot types, stable ids are pre-order (they shift on insertion, so they cannot key a breakpoint) and no sandbox or headless consumer executes a plan or interpreter program. `plan::RunControl` (null-default, separate from the observation-only `RunHooks`) makes every instr a safe point with cancel checked before and after it (new `RunError::Cancelled`); `plan::read_value` reads a result only from the safe point's own frame (`Available`, `NotYetComputed`, `OutOfScope`, `OptimizedAway` naming the CSE survivor, `NoSuchValue`); the interpreter gains `call_depth()` and stops a held op on cancel. `crd/ceir/inspect.hpp`'s `Session` binds one generation, resolves authored `file:line` breakpoints through DIAG.8a provenance (`Bound`/`NoCodeAtLine`/`UnknownFile`), pauses the executing thread, answers typed snapshots (IR type, canonical text, quantity dimension, host redaction) from the paused thread, steps into/over/out, resumes and cancels, refuses stale-generation and unbound requests before any work, and types its scope (`Task`, `WholeHost` with host freeze/thaw, `NonPausable` counting hits) and the `SameThread` refusal for a safe point on the controller's own thread. `test_inspect.cpp` (6 cases) drives the plan and the interpreter from a second thread after text, CSE, serialize and a fresh-Context load; `test_reload_inspect.cpp` (1) rebinds a moved line across a ReloadSet hot swap and refuses the other generation; teeth on the breakpoint sites, the frame boundary, the generation check, cancel at a pause, the rebind, the call depth, redaction and the held instr. **Pending jobs landed** (2026-10-07): `HostProvider::execute(..., session)` gives the submitting interpreter the session's safe points through `inspect::HostLink`; the provider's cancel flag is the execution's only flag (`Session::cancel` raises it, so pooled bodies stop too, and a pause polls it, so `request_cancel` ends a paused run); each stop reports `pending_jobs` (pooled launches not yet joined); bodies the provider runs on its own sub-interpreters (pooled launch bodies, parallel ranges, fold steps) are detached: a breakpoint there is counted and refused `DetachedBody`, never paused. `test_host_inspect.cpp` (3 cases, `[ceir][host][diag]`; the test thread is the pool's thread 0 and executes, a second thread controls, a backstop cancels a run a broken path would hang): a stop with a pooled launch pending, resume joining it (pending 1 then 0, result 23), 13 detached hits with an in-frame launch pausing, and a session cancel and a provider cancel at the pause ending a pooled body that cannot finish; teeth on the link raise, the pause poll, the detached hook, the pending probe, the interpreter's flag and the session attach. **GPU seam landed** (2026-10-07): `execute_lowered(..., DispatchSites*, DeviceInspect*)` attaches its recording to a session (`Session::begin_device`, refused before any work: `NotBound`, `StaleGeneration`, `Busy`, returned as `ExecuteError::InspectRefused` with nothing recorded) and reports each dispatch as a device point that never waits whatever the session's scope: a breakpoint bound to the dispatch op is counted (`device_hits`) and refused `NonPausable`, a pause request during a recording is refused `NonPausable`, and a cancel stops the recording before the next dispatch (`ExecuteError::Cancelled`, blamed on that dispatch's authored line). Decided gaps: a recording is exclusive (a host op recording device work mid-execution is refused `Busy`), and `execute_rt_lowered`/`execute_work_lowered`/the render executor take no session. `test_dispatch_inspect.cpp` (3 cases, device-free; the recording runs on a second thread under a `Task` session with a backstop) and one real-device case in `test_dispatch_provenance_vulkan.cpp` (Task session, hit refused `NonPausable`, validation clean, outputs `a + 2b`); teeth on the hold path, the cancel, the generation check, the count, the pause refusal, the detach and the exclusivity. **Inspect host and headless consumer landed** (2026-10-07): crd-ceir-cook's `InspectHost` cooks an authored text into a ReloadSet generation, compiles one entry, binds the session and runs the plan on its own executing thread (the consumer's thread is the controller); every install and start rebinds (a replaced generation is refused `StaleGeneration`), a load or start during an execution is refused `Busy`, a rejected or failed reload keeps the last good generation, and the destructor cancels and joins a paused run; `inspect::op_at_line` names a value by its authored line. The committed authored program `assets/ceir/inspect_demo.ceir` (anti-drift against its builder) is run by the headless consumer `ceridc inspect` (CLI-only; the agent transports' authority is DIAG.8c), which reports every stop's authored line and depth, the watched lines' typed values (`available`, `not-yet-computed`, `out-of-scope`, with type text and unit) and the scripted steps, bounds its stops and waits, and validates the request before running. `test_inspect_host.cpp` (4 cases) and `test_ceridc_inspect.cpp` (3 cases, in process and through the real binary); teeth on the load rebind, the start rebind, the destructor cancel, the step actions and the stop bound. The `DeviceInspect::refusal` out field is now reset when no session refuses (tooth: the stale value survived). **Sandbox consumer landed** (2026-10-07): `ceir` is a registered program folder (`engine://ceir/<name>` is `ceir/<name>.ceir`) and `SceneRenderer::resolve_program_text` exposes the renderer's app-first program read (`app://` shadows `engine://`, reporting which won); `crd-sandbox --inspect [id]` (with `--inspect-break`, `--inspect-watch`, `--inspect-step`, `--inspect-arg`, `--app-assets`) runs the authored program through a sandbox panel over the same `InspectHost`, keyed by the canonical folder/name so a reload that swaps the winning mount keeps the asset and its breakpoints. The frame loop is the controller: each frame polls for a stop with a zero timeout, takes the watched values once per new stop and draws a window (continue, step into/over/out, pause, cancel, run again, reload); a stop is new only by sequence, so a cancel accepted at a held stop is not read as another stop (found by the new test). `crd-sandbox-inspect-tests` (5 cases, 164 assertions; device-free, compiles the panel source with a SceneRenderer for its mounts) shows the app shadow and the mount-swapping reload, frames ticking while the program is held and while it runs (each tick far under its bound), a breakpoint stop with typed and unavailable values, step into/out and continue, a pause request landing in the running loop and a cancel, a scripted run, `Busy` while held, `StaleGeneration` for the replaced generation, and destruction while held; teeth on the polling tick, the scheme order, the folder registration, the per-stop refresh, the sequence check and the canonical identity. On this machine's GPU the sandbox ran the committed program in its frame loop (breakpoint, step into, step out, finished 36 over a 4 s smoke) and, with an application file shadowing it, held at a breakpoint for 167 presented frames. **All local clauses are shown**; the row waits only for its first hosted run, which must show `crd-sandbox-inspect-tests` (5 cases, 164 assertions), `crd-ceir-tests` (548 cases), `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (46), `crd-ceir-host-tests` (32), `crd-ceir-gpu-tests` (140) and `crd-ceridc-tests` (5) green on all six lanes with their `diag 8b` cases passing, `crd-render-asset-core-tests` and `crd-scene-render-tests` still green, `crd-sandbox` building on every lane, and `crd-ceir-gpu-vulkan-tests`' `diag 8b` case passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes (it skips on the hosted Windows lanes). **2026-10-08 CI repair:** an inspected run keeps its 2^24-step budget, so the sandbox panel test now checks the frame loop over one whole fuel-bound run and repeats a pause until it lands in the loop, instead of assuming 20 frames outlast the run. **Done (2026-10-12):** `crd-sandbox-inspect-tests` (24 cases on Windows, 23 on Linux), `crd-ceir-tests`, `crd-ceir-cook-tests`, `crd-ceir-host-tests`, `crd-ceir-gpu-tests` and `crd-ceridc-tests` (22) are green on all six lanes with their 26 `diag 8b` cases, `crd-render-asset-core-tests` and `crd-scene-render-tests` are green (on win-asan the latter fails only its two registered TP-1 DX12 ray-tracing gates), `crd-sandbox` builds on every lane, and the Vulkan `diag 8b` case passes with 41 assertions on lavapipe, in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88`; | 085 | <a id="slice-diag.8c"></a>**DIAG.8c** | Done | Typed bounded human/agent diagnostic commands with separate inspect/record/inject authority; DG13/DG17. **Command service and transports landed** (2026-10-07): crd-perf's `DiagCommandService` is the one service every consumer calls. The host grants authority classes when it builds it (`read`, `record`, `inject`, `remote-enable`, `upload`, `process-memory`; none implies another; never in a request, so neither a request nor an asset can raise it); every refusal comes before any work in a fixed order (schema, unknown command, authority, bounds `oversized`, arguments `bad-argument`/no root `unavailable`, stale cursor, cancellation) and leaves the retained snapshot alone; pagination cuts every page from one retained snapshot by `generation * 2^20 + offset`, so the same cursor gives the same bytes and a new snapshot makes old cursors stale; pages, items and strings are byte-bounded and say when clipped. Built-ins: `diag.commands`, `diag.capabilities` (doctor modes and dependencies, authority classes), `jobs.waits` (parked fibers' wait edges, worker responsiveness), `memory.allocators`, `bundle.inspect` (bounded non-executing importer; the file's size checked before a byte is read), `capture.start`/`capture.stop` (`record`; a CPROF file created exclusively under the root); evidence that cannot exist answers `unavailable` with its reason. Upper modules add commands through `register_command` with the same checks. `ceridc diag` and the MCP `diag` tool bind a service built from the process's start-up flags (default `read`); the tool's arguments are the request fields only. `test_diag_commands.cpp` (9 cases on profiling builds, 8 with profiling compiled out; the executable links no ceridc, MCP or network code: the process-local proof) shows the refusal order with handler and file-byte counters unchanged, deterministic pages and stale cursors, the byte bounds, distinct authority classes, the bundle, capture, allocator and wait-graph commands; `test_ceridc_diag.cpp` (3 cases) shows native, verb, in-process MCP and the real binary (CLI and MCP stdio) returning the same bytes, a smuggled `grant` refused, and malformed counts as protocol faults. Teeth on the authority check, the read-before-bound, the cursor generation, the schema check, the page byte bound, a refusal discarding the snapshot, a transport taking the grant from its arguments and a capture answering `ok` with profiling compiled out. **Program provenance landed** (2026-10-07): `program.provenance` (`read`, a path under the host root) is registered by crd-ceir-cook, not crd-ceir, because the core's link edges stay the host-only substrate (I5) and the cook bridge already loads every program form; crd-ceir-cook now declares and links crd-perf. The file's size is bounded before a byte is read; a cooked CRDR program, a raw CEIR binary or CEIR text (parsed under the request's relative path, so the root never appears) loads into a fresh Context with the host's dialects registered first (an unregistered op answers `unregistered`, never non-intrinsic); a load failure is refused `failed` naming the text's file:line:col, the binary's offset or the cooked read error. Each op is one pre-order `op` item (stable id, name, depth, closest-known file:line:col, gap, first CHIR origin with its id and position, native binding, the rendered provenance last), and an op whose origins the item cannot state (several, or one naming another op) is followed by one `origin` item per origin, so a merged origin is never lost to a clipped string (found by the CHIR test: `state_update commit` is the second origin of its state cell). ceridc's CLI and MCP services both bind it through `bind_diag_commands`. `test_program_diag.cpp` (2 cases, 199 assertions: the committed `inspect_demo.ceir` as text, binary and cooked, matching a text-scanning oracle item for item; no registrar; refusals with the command's run and byte counters at zero; an oversized file unread; a parse failure at its scanned line and column), a `[chir][diag]` case naming four authored CHIR nodes of the cooked `event_handler.chir` at their scanned positions, and the ceridc parity cases extended to the command (native, verb, in-process MCP, the real binary's CLI and MCP stdio byte-equal); teeth on the size bound, the relative path, the registrar, the nested walk, the origin items and both ceridc bindings. Its hosted run must also show `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (48 cases), `crd-chir-tests` (30) and `crd-ceridc-tests` (8) green on all six lanes. **GPU resource summaries landed** (2026-10-07): `gpu.resources` (`read`, no path) is registered by crd-perf-gpu-bridge, not by crd-gpu-context as this row first said, because crd-gpu-context may not link crd-perf and the bridge is the one module naming both; the host owns a `GpuResourcesCommand` listing the contexts and frame graphs to summarize (eight of each). It answers the process-wide identity registry's live resource, program and pass counts, each registered context's backend, adapter, validity and validation-mode states, and each registered frame graph's transient bytes after and before aliasing, budget refusal and last execute's barrier, submit, pass, async-pass and present counts; heap usage is an `unavailable` item (no backend queries heap usage or budget), as is a missing context or frame graph. ceridc binds it with no context into its CLI and MCP services. `test_gpu_resources_diag.cpp` (2 cases, device-free: identities moving by exactly what the test minted, fake contexts' and graph's fields, removal, refusals before any run, deterministic pages, the list bound), a real-device case in `crd-gpu-context-vulkan-tests` (a storage buffer moves the live count by one; the context's own adapter and activation; two disjoint equal transients alias to half their logical bytes; validation clean) and the ceridc parity cases extended to the command (native, verb, in-process MCP and the real binary byte-equal); teeth on the registry read, the unavailable item, the context list, a frame-graph field, removal and the ceridc binding. Its hosted run must also show `crd-perf-gpu-bridge-tests` (14 cases with profiling, 2 with it compiled out) and `crd-ceridc-tests` (8) green on all six lanes and the Vulkan case passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes (it skips on the hosted Windows lanes). **Program inspection under Execute landed** (2026-10-07): running an authored program under a debug session is its own authority class, `execute` (no other class implies it; the row's "inspect" class is `read`). A request may carry named arguments (`DiagArg`), taken only by a command registered with an argument check: the service bounds their count, name and value (`oversized`) and refuses a malformed or repeated name, arguments to a command without a check, and the command's own refusal (`bad-argument`), all in the arguments step before the cursor and cancel. crd-ceir-cook registers `program.inspect` (`execute`, a path and named arguments: entry, args, breaks, watches, steps, max_stops up to the host's limit): the authored text is cooked under the request's relative path and run through `crd/ceir/cook/inspect_script.hpp`, the one scripted-inspection engine `ceridc inspect` now also uses, answering a `breakpoint` item per breakpoint, a `stop` item per stop (authored file:line:col, depth, op, action) followed by one `value` item per watched line, and a `result` item per result; the caller's cancel flag is polled while the program runs and at every stop, a cancel that arrives before the executing thread attached is repeated until the session takes it, and every wait is bounded. The agent transport reaches it only through the MCP `diag` tool (`args`, an object of strings) under the process's start-up grant (`ceridc mcp --diag-grant read,execute`); `ceridc diag --param name=value` is the CLI form; `ceridc inspect` stays the command line's convenience report and is not an MCP tool. `test_diag_commands.cpp` gains the argument case (12 refusals with the command's check and handler counters, the order against authority, the cursor and the cancel flag, the listing's `takes_args`) and the `execute` class in the authority and capabilities cases; `test_inspect_diag.cpp` (3 cases, 218 assertions: the committed `inspect_demo.ceir` stepped into and out with typed values at scanned positions and pages cut from one run; authority, 14 argument, path and cancel refusals with nothing read or started; an oversized file unread; a cook failure at its line and a missing entry; the stop bound, a scripted cancel, the caller's cancel at a stop and while the program runs); `test_ceridc_diag.cpp` (2 new cases: native, verb and in-process MCP byte-equal under `read,execute` and all refused under `read`, malformed `args` as protocol faults; the real binary's CLI `--param` and MCP stdio byte-equal to native, refused without the grant). Teeth on the authority class, arguments to a command without a check, the argument step's place before the cursor, the cancel poll, the cancel retry, the stop bound, the relative cook path, the MCP and CLI argument channels and the ceridc binding. Its hosted run must also show `crd-perf-tests` with the diag-command cases (10 with profiling, 9 with it compiled out), `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (51 cases) and `crd-ceridc-tests` (10) green on all six lanes. **Replay preparation landed** (2026-10-07): `replay.prepare` (`read`, a path) is registered by crd-ceir-cook and loads a program exactly as `program.provenance` does (one private loader now serves both). It prepares and never runs: one item per replay input in a fixed order with its DIAG.9a guarantee (`program`, `build` identity; `entry-arguments`, `random`, `clock`, `host-state`, `external-results` event; `schedule`; `device-tolerance` numeric, never a bit-identity claim), whether the program needs it from each op's effective effects (a `func.call` resolved through the module's symbol table to its callee's, with a per-op cycle guard), the first op needing it at its authored file:line:col, and the reason it is missing; an opaque op (unregistered, or with ExternalCall effects) needs external results and makes every other effect-derived need `unknown` (missing), never "not needed". Only the content hash exists, so the summary says `replay: unavailable` and lists the missing inputs, with the weakest determinism class claimed and the unclaimed op count. Bundles carry no program or input sections, so no bundle form was added. ceridc's CLI and MCP services bind it. `test_replay_diag.cpp` (4 cases, 171 assertions: the committed `inspect_demo.ceir` in three forms needing only identity and entry arguments; a specimen whose callee, defined after its caller and called twice, draws a random number and reads the scene, blamed on the first call at its scanned position; an unregistered op making needs unknown; refusals with nothing run or read, an oversized file unread, a parse failure at its scanned position) and the ceridc parity cases extended to the command (native, verb, in-process MCP, the real binary's CLI and MCP stdio byte-equal); teeth on the symbol-table resolution, the per-op cycle guard, the opaque rule, the size bound, the relative path and the ceridc binding. Its hosted run must also show `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (55 cases) and `crd-ceridc-tests` (10) green on all six lanes and `crd-chir-tests` (30) still green. **GUI consumer landed** (2026-10-07): crd-perf-ui's `DiagCommandPanel` is a form over the service's own request (a command from the copied listing, a path, `name=value` argument lines, page bounds) and a view of its own response document; it keeps the service's `DiagResult` unchanged and adds no command, check or authority (the grant is the host's). Requests run on the panel's worker thread, so a frame only polls (`tick`) and never takes the service's lock; one request at a time (`Busy`); `cancel` raises the running request's flag, so the GUI can end a running command, and destroying the panel cancels and joins it; the next page sends the snapshot's own request with its next cursor, and an old cursor shows the service's `stale-cursor` refusal; items are split from the document structurally, never re-serialized. crd-sandbox builds its service from start-up flags only (`--diag-grant`, default `read`; `--diag-root`), serves the ceridc set with its real GPU context registered for `gpu.resources` (no frame graph: the worker thread calls the service), draws the panel beside the profiler and `--diag <command>` (with `--diag-path`, `--diag-param`) sends one request at start and logs every page; an unknown command refuses to run. `test_diag_panel.cpp` (5 cases in `crd-perf-ui-tests`, `[diag-panel]`, device-free, no ImGui context): every page byte-equal to a native call at the same cursor, the listing, items across five pages with a label holding JSON structure, the stale refusal; the host's grant (`unauthorized` with no handler run), the panel's own form errors and the service's argument and bound refusals passed through; frames ticking while a command holds the service's lock, `Busy`, and the panel's cancel ending it; destruction cancelling and joining; the splitter on strings, nesting and malformed documents. Teeth on the next-page cursor, the polling tick, the cancel flag, the destructor's cancel, the listing copy, the string-aware splitter, the form check and `page_at`'s cursor. On this machine's GPU the sandbox answered `gpu.resources` (its Vulkan context and adapter), `program.provenance`, `replay.prepare`, `memory.allocators`, `jobs.waits`, `program.inspect` refused `unauthorized` under `read` and run to 36 under `read,execute`, inside smoke runs that passed. **All local clauses are shown**; the row waits only for its first hosted run, which must show `crd-perf-tests` with the diag-command cases (10 with profiling, 9 with it compiled out), `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (55 cases), `crd-chir-tests` (30), `crd-ceridc-tests` (10, including the real binary's CLI and MCP stdio answers), `crd-perf-gpu-bridge-tests` (14 cases with profiling, 2 with it compiled out) and `crd-perf-ui-tests` (21 cases with profiling, 14 with it compiled out, its 5 `diag-panel` cases on every lane) green on all six lanes, the `diag gpu.resources` case in `crd-gpu-context-vulkan-tests` passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes (it skips on the hosted Windows lanes), and `ceridc` and `crd-sandbox` building on every lane. Not claimed: the synchronous MCP stdio loop does not process `notifications/cancelled` (the service's cancel flag is shown natively and from the GUI); the window's buttons are not driven by a test (they call the panel methods the test drives). **Done (2026-10-12):** `crd-perf-tests` with its diag-command cases (now 12 with profiling, 11 with it compiled out), `crd-ceir-cook-tests`, `crd-chir-tests`, `crd-ceridc-tests` (22), `crd-perf-gpu-bridge-tests` (14 with profiling, 2 without) and `crd-perf-ui-tests` (21 with profiling, 14 without, its 5 `diag panel` cases on every lane) are green on all six lanes, and the `diag gpu.resources` Vulkan case passes with 85 assertions on lavapipe, in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88`; [audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md#rows-closed-on-7a958b88-2026-10-12). **Owners** (2026-10-25, [DIAG.12](#slice-diag.12)): `notifications/cancelled` [AGENT.2](#slice-agent.2), the window's buttons [I2D-9.e](#slice-i2d-9.e). | [DIAG.8b](#slice-diag.8b) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-8c); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [command service and transports](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8c-command-service-and-transports.md); [program provenance](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8c-program-provenance-command.md); [GPU resource summaries](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8c-gpu-resource-summaries.md); [program inspection under Execute](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8c-program-inspect-under-execute.md); [replay preparation](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8c-replay-preparation.md); [GUI consumer](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8c-gui-consumer.md); [run 37776561255 repairs](sessions/2026-10-08-complete-run-37776561255-repairs.md); [Complete run 2026-10-12](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326); [Needs CI audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md) |
| 086 | <a id="slice-diag.9a"></a>**DIAG.9a** | Done | Recorded inputs, random/time/external-completion identity, asset generations and declared replay envelopes; DG15. **Run records and replay landed** (2026-10-08): measured first that no CEIR op reads a random stream, clock, host state or external completion (none was added just to be recorded) and that the compiled-plan executor already offers a safe point per instr with frame-local value reads. `crd/ceir/cook/replay_record.hpp` (crd-ceir-cook) defines an immutable, versioned, checksummed run record: the build (version, platform, compiler, arch, configuration, asserts, executor version), the program as its cooked blob with its content hash and authored path, the entry and arguments, each replay input's need and state (from the same effect walk as `replay.prepare`; an input nothing captures is stored `missing`, never assumed), a bounded trace (one event per dispatched instr: op stable id, call depth, up to four results read at the frame's next safe point; `max_events` 4096 by default, at most 65536, with the total counted) and the outcome (run error, faulting op, results, cells). Replay loads the record's own blob, never the checkout's file, and reports the first divergence in a fixed order (path, value, length, outcome, results, cells); an incompatible record is refused before anything runs (not a record, another schema, truncated or bad checksum, a blob that is not its recorded content: `failed`; another build unless `build=any`, or a missing input its program needs: `unavailable`, naming the fields or inputs). Commands: `replay.record` (`execute`, also `record`: crd-perf's new second authority class `DiagCommandSpec::also`; creates the record exclusively, refusing an existing file before it reads or runs anything), `replay.run` (`execute`; `program=` replays the same inputs against an edited file explicitly, `build=any`) and `replay.prepare` now also reading a record; ceridc's CLI and MCP services and crd-sandbox bind them. The committed `assets/ceir/replay_demo.ceir` fails `selector-out-of-range` on the seeded argument `main(1)`. `test_replay_record.cpp` (7 cases, 541 assertions): the failure recorded with its artifact, build, arguments and every event, deterministic bytes; after the program file is edited a fresh host reproduces it from the blob, and replaying against the edit names the edited constant at its scanned line (recorded 1, observed -1); ten incompatible records refused with nothing run; the trace bound with loss counted and a later edit still caught as an outcome; both authority classes and the argument refusals; `replay.prepare` on a record; every divergence kind in order. `test_ceridc_diag.cpp` gains the process-restart case: one `ceridc` process records, the file is edited, a second reproduces and a third names the divergence, each byte-equal to the native call and to the MCP stdio tool text. `test_diag_commands.cpp` gains the second-class case. Teeth on the value and read-count comparisons, loading from the checkout, the build check, the trace bound, the checksum, the second class, the missing-input check, the content-hash check, the value reads, the early overwrite refusal, the ceridc binding and `replay.prepare`'s record states. **Recording at the inspect host landed** (2026-10-08): `inspect::Session::run` takes a safe-point observer called before the session decides whether to stop (its `Cancel` ends the run), and `ReplayRecorder` (`replay_record.hpp`, the trace `run_traced` takes) is that observer, so breakpoints, steps and value reads leave the trace exactly as an unobserved run's. `InspectHost::start(args, HostRecording)` re-cooks the installed generation's module into the record's blob on the controller before the executing thread starts (valid only at that generation's own content hash) and classifies the inputs with `replay.record`'s walk; `InspectHost::record` gives the record once the run has ended (`running`, `cancelled` and `not-recorded` give none). Record schema 2 adds the asset id and the ReloadSet generation that ran (0 and 0 without a reloading host; a generation without an asset is malformed; schema 1 is refused); a run cannot span a reload on the host (a load is refused while attached), so a record names one generation and still holds it after the host reloads. `replay.run` answers both; `write_record_file` creates a record exclusively. Consumers: `ceridc inspect --record <file>` (an existing file refused before anything runs; a cancelled run writes nothing) and crd-sandbox `--inspect-record <file>` (the panel's frame loop holds and steps the run; written when it ends). `test_host_record.cpp` (4 cases): `main(1)` of `replay_demo.ceir` stopped at two breakpoints, stepped into and out, records the same inputs, events, outcome, results and cells as `replay.record`'s unobserved run, names the host's asset and generation, replays its blob in a fresh Context at the switch's scanned position and is reproduced by `replay.run` from the file; after a hot reload the record still holds generation 1 (its hash and failure) and replaying it against the edit names the edited constant at its scanned line; a start without recording forgets it; a held run is `running`, a cancelled one `cancelled`; schema 1 and a generation without an asset are refused; an observer sees every safe point and its `Cancel` ends a session's run. `test_ceridc_inspect.cpp` gains the `--record` case (in process and the real binary, each record reproduced by another `ceridc` process's `replay.run`; the overwrite refusal; a cancelled run unwritten); `crd-sandbox-inspect-tests` gains the frame-loop case. Teeth on the observer call, observing only unstopped safe points, the record's generation taken at read time, a cancelled run recorded, sticky recording, the generation-without-asset check, the observer's Cancel, the ceridc `--record` and overwrite checks and the panel dropping the recording. **Recording at the host provider landed** (2026-10-08): measured first that `HostProvider` builds a fresh submitting interpreter per `execute` (no cell survives one, and no production host keeps an interpreter across invokes) and that its only program-observable schedule choices are its settings: the job split (results never depend on it: state-free bodies, index-ordered outputs and folds, the lowest failing index wins; a race answers its first operand) and the per-body step budget. Record schema 3 names the executor (`plan` or `host`) and, for a host record, the job split, step budget and interpreter error; the decoder refuses mixed-executor fields (`malformed`) and schemas 1 and 2. `cook::InterpreterRecorder` traces the submitting interpreter (pre hook: op stable id and call depth; post hook, after a successful dispatch: up to four results) and keeps the cells in stable id order; `HostProvider::execute(..., HostObserver)` attaches it before the entry and detaches it after every pooled launch was joined, and sub-interpreters (parallel ranges, folds, pooled launch bodies) carry no hooks, so their work is seen at the joining op. `crd/ceir/host/host_replay.hpp` (crd-ceir-host now links crd-ceir-cook, which stays jobs-free): `record_host_run` stores the schedule input `recorded`; `replay_host_record` refuses a plan record, another build (unless allowed), a missing input or a blob that is not its content before anything runs, then replays the record's own blob in a fresh Context with the recorded schedule and names the first divergence and the fault at their authored positions; `replay.run` refused a host record (until the host executor below). `test_host_replay.cpp` (4 cases in `crd-ceir-host-tests`: a pooled launch calling a function, a looping map_reduce, a state cell and an argument-guarded loop; the same events on 1, 2, 3, 8 and 16 jobs and none from a body; encode/decode/re-encode byte-equal and a fresh-Context replay on the recorded and on an explicit job split; `main(0)`'s `BadForStep` at its scanned line; a 16-step budget's `FuelExhausted` reproduced only through the recorded budget; an edited launched constant named at the await's scanned line, recorded 25, observed 36; refusals with nothing traced and six mixed-executor records refused by the decoder), the `replay.run` host-record refusal and the schema 1 and 2 refusals. Teeth (5, win-debug) in the note. Observed: the real sandbox records the program through `--diag`, the record is byte-equal to ceridc's record of the same file, and a second sandbox process and ceridc reproduce it (ceridc also on 16 jobs); a third sandbox process names the edit. **Boundaries, not local work:** state cells across invokes and a run spanning a reload wait for a host that keeps one interpreter across invokes (only tests call `migrate_state`); external I/O completions and network or physical effects stubbed only in explicit test replay wait for an op that declares an I/O, external-call, agent or physics-write effect (none does). Until then a program needing external results keeps them missing and its record is refused before anything runs (`test_replay_diag.cpp`, `test_replay_record.cpp`), so nothing is silently rerun. **All local clauses are shown**; the row waits only for its first hosted run (Needs CI once a published run carries this work), which must show `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (103 cases, with the 9 random-input, 10 `[clock]`, 9 `[event]` and 9 `[device]` cases), `crd-ceir-host-tests` (47, with the 11 host-record, 2 host-input, 1 host-clock and 1 host-event cases), `crd-ceridc-tests` (20, with the real binary's cross-process plan, host, seeded-random, clock, event and device replays, `inspect --record`, `inspect --seed`, the `inspect` clock flags and `inspect --events`), `crd-sandbox-inspect-tests` (24 on Windows, 23 elsewhere, with the seeded, clocked and 3 `[event]` panel cases, the 2 `[host]` cases and the `[device]` cases: the Vulkan one passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes and skipping without a Vulkan device, the DX12 one passing or skipping on the Windows lanes), `crd-ceir-gpu-tests` (140), `crd-perf-tests` with the diag-command cases (11 with profiling, 10 with it compiled out), `crd-chir-tests` (30), `crd-ceir-tests` (567, with the 16 seam cases and the generated input smoke cases) and `crd-perf-ui-tests` (21 with profiling, 14 without) green on all six lanes, `crd-ceir-gpu-vulkan-tests` (60) with its `diag 9a` device case (record, replay and the command leg) passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes (it skips on the hosted Windows lanes, which have no Vulkan device), `crd-ceir-gpu-dx12-tests` (47) green on the Windows lanes with its `diag 9a` device case passing or skipping without a D3D12 device, the `crd-ceir-opgen-drift` ctest green, and `ceridc`, `crd-sandbox`, `crd-gpu-context-vulkan-tests` and `crd-gpu-context-dx12-tests` building on every lane where they exist. Count updated 2026-10-10: `crd-ceir-cook-tests` now has 105 cases, DIAG.9b having added two `[diag9b]` external I/O replay cases ([session](sessions/2026-10-10-diag-9b-external-io-replay.md)). 2026-10-11: the two `test_replay_device.cpp` cases that wrote `diag9a_device_record.crpl` now write separate files: as separate CTest processes in one directory they shared the file, the likely cause of one failure of "a device run on the CPU reference is recorded and reproduces exactly from its file" in a full win-debug run, whose output was not kept ([session](sessions/2026-10-11-diag-10a-host-parallel-range.md)). **Done (2026-10-12):** every named suite is green on all six lanes in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88` (`crd-ceir-cook-tests` now 115 cases, `crd-ceir-host-tests` 55, `crd-ceridc-tests` 22, `crd-ceir-tests` 576, the diag-command cases 12 and 11), the `diag 9a` Vulkan device case passes with 851 assertions on lavapipe, `crd-ceir-gpu-dx12-tests` (47) is green on the Windows lanes, the sandbox Vulkan device case passes on lavapipe, and `crd-ceir-opgen-drift` passes on every lane; | 087 | <a id="slice-diag.9b"></a>**DIAG.9b** | Done | Triggerable bounded flight recording and authorized fault injection across load/execute/reload/teardown; DG15. **Flight recorder landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): `crd/perf/flight_recorder.hpp` keeps fixed 152-byte events in a ring preallocated from a validated, versioned `FlightPolicy` (pre + post <= capacity, so the post window never overwrites the pre window; last known good on a rejected or busy reload). A trigger takes only its own mutex; the post window is anchored under the ring mutex at the first event that sees it and closes at `post_events`, `post_window_ns`, shutdown or the fatal path; later triggers are suppressed and counted with their kinds. A capture carries the trigger and its scalars, file:line and message, the retained events, loss metadata (outside the pre window, post truncated, ring unavailable, dropped while waiting, suppressed) and the replay recipe registered at the trigger or the reason replay is unavailable; a Fatal capture is terminal. It is bundle section `FlightRecord` (tag 6, bounded decoder, named by `bundle.inspect`). Trigger sources on existing seams (`crd/perf/flight_triggers.hpp`): crd's assert handler and the fatal-invariant handler (both chained; new `get_fatal_invariant_handler`/`default_fatal_invariant`), the jobs watchdog's starvation and hang handlers, host frame boundaries (`FlightFrameWatch`), an `IAllocator` decorator and a `StreamingAllocator` residency-policy decorator. 14 `[diag9b]` cases in crd-perf-tests (each source on its real seam, the fatal path in a child specimen whose bundle is read back, a concurrency case that found and now guards a post-window race) green on win-debug, win-shipping, win-clang-cl-shipping, win-asan and the three WSL lanes; teeth shown. **Seeded fault injection landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): `crd/perf/fault_injection.hpp` arms a validated, versioned `FaultPlan` (seed, up to 8 rules: site, hit range, probability ppm, value; a round-tripping text form) only for a grant holding `Inject` and only in a build with fault seams; firing is a pure function of seed/site/rule/hit. Sites on existing seams: `allocation-failure` (`FaultAllocator`, `try_allocate` only) and `failed-read`/`partial-read`/`delayed-completion`/`cancelled-completion` on crd-core's new I/O fault seam (`crd/core/fault_seam.hpp`), which `read_file_binary`, `read_file_range` and the `AsyncFile` completion consult. A fired fault is recorded into the flight recorder and fires the new `InjectedFault` trigger with a recipe whose one-hit plan reproduces it alone; while armed the plan owns the recorder's recipe, and disarming marks replay unavailable. Shipping builds compile the seam out (`CRD_FAULT_SEAMS` from `_crd_shipping`): every arm is Unavailable. `fault.arm`/`fault.disarm` (Inject) and `fault.status` (Read) commands. Found and fixed: a short or failed `AsyncFile` completion silently zero-filled its buffer and the streamed load handed those bytes to the loader; `read_async` now reports `AsyncReadStatus` and the load fails first. Shown: a reload interrupted by a MESH-loader allocation failure, a failed or a short pack read keeps the last good payload; a short, failed or cancelled streamed completion ends Failed without reaching the loader; a delayed one loads. 7 `[fault]` cases in crd-perf-tests, 2 `[diag9b]` in crd-platform-tests and 2 in crd-resources-tests green on win-debug, win-shipping and win-clang-cl-shipping (compiled-out branch), win-asan and the three WSL lanes; teeth shown. **Device-loss simulation landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): a second crd-core seam, the GPU fault seam (`install_gpu_fault_hook`/`consult_gpu_fault`, compiled out with the I/O seam in shipping), is consulted by Vulkan's `vk_submit` and DX12's `dx12_submit` before anything is queued and only on a device not already lost (never at a wait: Vulkan cannot stop queued work); the new `device-loss` site (`FaultInjector::gpu_hook`) answers `LoseDevice` at the plan's submission. Vulkan then records `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` as `Simulated` and takes the DIAG.7c(g) loss path; DX12 calls `ID3D12Device5::RemoveDevice`, reads DRED and records the new `Dx12RemovalOrigin::Simulated` (removal bundle version 3). Shown on each provider: the frames before the lost submission and the fault in the flight capture, the `InjectedFault` trigger at the site and hit, the loss/removal record and its bundle labelled Simulated, the context latched, the one-hit plan from the recipe reproducing the loss alone on a fresh device, and a fresh device working once the hook is removed. 1 new `[fault]` case in crd-perf-tests, 1 `[diag9b]` case each in crd-gpu-context-vulkan-tests and crd-gpu-context-dx12-tests (both full suites green in one process on win-debug); teeth shown. **Authored policy and plans, flight commands and doctor modes landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): `crd/perf/diag_assets.hpp` parses the flight policy and fault plans as versioned TOML documents (crd-perf now links crd-toml); every refusal (unknown key, wrong type, negative or oversized integer, unknown trigger, kind, version, invalid TOML, a policy or plan its own validator refuses) names the key and leaves the output untouched; the committed `assets/diag/flight_default.flight.toml` is held equal to `FlightPolicy{}` and `assets/diag/allocation_failure_once.fault.toml` arms through `fault.arm asset=<path>` (also the route for plans over the 512-byte argument bound). `FlightCommandHost` is the recorder's sink and keeps the last capture; `flight.status` (Read), `flight.trigger`, `flight.policy` (last known good on any refusal) and `flight.write` (never overwrites) (Record). The doctor reports `flight-recorder` and `fault-injection` live (`DoctorLive`, and `diag.capabilities` through `DiagServiceConfig`). 7 cases in crd-perf-tests green on win-debug, win-shipping and win-clang-cl-shipping (compiled-out branches), win-asan and the three WSL lanes; teeth shown. **Build and symbol identity in a flight bundle landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): `write_flight_bundle(capture, modules)` adds a `BuildIdentity` section (tag 7, `crd/perf/build_identity.hpp`: version, platform, compiler and version, arch, config, asserts/profiling/fault-seams/ASan/TSan flags; compile-time constants, bounded decoder, `same_build_identity` names each differing field; `bundle.inspect` reports `same_as_host`) and a SymbolIndex of the host's module snapshot, or an explicit absent SymbolIndex the manifest declares; writing never enumerates, so the fatal path writes a snapshot taken at start. `enumerate_loaded_modules` is the live counterpart of the DIAG.5d minidump parser (Windows: pinned Toolhelp modules, in-memory PE headers bounds-checked, RSDS GUID/age; Linux: `dl_iterate_phdr`, PT_LOAD span, GNU build-id; basenames only; 1024 bound); `merge_module_snapshot` keeps unloaded generations; `FlightCommandHost::refresh_modules` holds the snapshot and `flight.write` refreshes it. The importer decodes tag 7 and no longer counts an absent SymbolIndex as symbols. Shown against oracles sharing no code with the walk: an OS-written minidump of the same process (Windows) and each module's file on disk (both), a freed DLL kept unloaded with its identity, and the fatal specimen's bundle naming its own executable and this lane's build. 8 `[identity]` cases on Windows (7 on Linux) plus extended fatal, inspect and `flight.write` cases in crd-perf-tests and two new fuzz seeds (12) green on win-debug (whole tree), win-shipping, win-clang-cl-shipping, win-asan and the three WSL lanes; teeth shown, including clang-cl's `-Wswitch` on a missed tag. **Cook-stage injection landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): the asset processor (crd-cooker's `cmd_cook`) is the cook stage; `read_file_text` now consults the I/O fault seam too, and an injected read's flight event names the file by its last path component only. Unreadable is no longer absent: an input that exists but cannot be read fails the cook whatever the handler returned (`CookIO::unreadable`), an unreadable `.meta` stops the cook and keeps the asset's id, and `stable_id` never mints over an unreadable sidecar; before this a transient read failure re-minted the asset's or sub-product's id (breaking every reference) and an optional input was cooked as absent and published. Shown: a cook interrupted by a failed or short read of the source, the id `.meta` or an existing input publishes nothing (pack, cached artifact, cook database and id kept byte for byte; the capture names the file; the recipe's one-hit plan reproduces each alone) and the next clean cook publishes the new content under the same id; a read fault in the up-to-date check recooks byte-identical bytes, never a stale product. 4 `[cook]` cases in crd-resources-tests, 1 new `[fault]` case in crd-perf-tests and an extended crd-platform-tests case green on win-debug (whole tree), win-shipping, win-clang-cl-shipping, win-asan and the three WSL lanes; teeth shown. **Install-stage and teardown injection landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): install is `ResourceManager::mount_manifest` (a cooked pack over the mounted ones) and `SceneRenderer::reload` (a render asset into the renderer's live slot), both already on the I/O fault seam; an install interrupted by a failed or short read installs nothing (the mounted pack keeps serving the id with its payload's pointer, generation and bytes; the renderer keeps its live frame and generation), the capture names the file and the recipe's one-hit plan interrupts the same install alone. Found and fixed: a pack torn on disk (cut inside its artifacts) mounted, its entries shadowed the earlier mount's and every later load of them failed; `mount_manifest` now refuses a manifest naming bytes past the pack's end before touching the live table. Execute is the device-loss slice (injection at a live submission). Teardown performs no faultable read or recoverable allocation (checked with every site armed and hooked), returns every allocation of a run in which every fault kind fired (a retired payload included), drains a delayed completion in flight, and the recorder delivers its open window truncated with the later faults suppressed. 3 cases in crd-resources-tests (`[install]` x2, `[teardown]`) and 1 in crd-scene-render-tests (`[install]`, now linking crd-perf) green on win-debug, win-shipping and win-clang-cl-shipping (refusal branches), win-asan and the three WSL lanes; teeth shown. **External I/O replay refused landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): no build replays external I/O, since a run record holds no external I/O completions or opaque call results. `check_external_replay` (crd-ceir-cook) decides from the ops of the program a replay would run, never from the record's claims, and `replay.run` refuses Unavailable before anything runs on every executor: the record's own program once its hash is checked and the `program=` one before its plan compiles (plan), both before the host executor is called (host), both in `replay_device_record` (device); the refusal names the program and its first file-reading op at file:line:col. `record_missing_inputs` counts `external-results` missing unless the record says not needed and not held, and `replay.prepare` never answers it available from a record. (2026-10-10, DIAG.10a: a record file claiming them held, or with a state its need contradicts, is now refused as it is decoded; `record_missing_inputs` still guards records held in memory, and the `[diag9b]` cases check both routes.) Before this a record claiming the results recorded, or claiming them not needed while its program read a file, replayed. The doctor reports `external-io-replay` never compiled, enabled or usable, in any build (`diag.capabilities` too). 1 `[diag9b]` case in each of `test_replay_record.cpp` and `test_replay_device.cpp` (crd-ceir-cook-tests, with a test op declaring FileIO and a stub host executor) and the extended doctor case in crd-perf-tests green on win-debug (whole tree), win-shipping, win-clang-cl-shipping, win-asan and the three WSL lanes; teeth shown. **Sandbox host landed locally** (2026-10-10, committed, not pushed): crd-sandbox is a real flight and fault host (`sandbox/src/flight_host.{hpp,cpp}`). Every run starts the recorder with the default policy or an authored asset (`--flight-policy`; a refused asset or a missing `--flight-dir` refuses the run) and installs the assertion, fatal-invariant, jobs starvation and hang (with a 250 ms watchdog window the host gives the pool), frame-hitch and resource-manager allocation-failure triggers (no `StreamingAllocator` exists in the sandbox, so resource pressure has no seam there); its sink is a `FlightCommandHost` whose module snapshot is refreshed after the GPU device is up, and with `--flight-dir` each capture is written as `flight-<n>.cdb` (never overwriting, newest 10 kept) by a writer safe on a fatal path (bounded owner-aware locks, the new `FlightCommandHost::try_modules`). With `--diag` the service serves `flight.*` and `fault.*`, the doctor reports the live recorder and injector and the I/O and GPU fault hooks are installed. A smoke failure now takes the ordered teardown: before, an injected device loss reached that path and the scope exit destroyed the device before its children and crashed. 7 `[flight-host]` cases in `crd-sandbox-inspect-tests` (31 cases on Windows, 30 on Linux) green on win-debug (whole tree), win-shipping and win-clang-cl-shipping (refusal branches), win-asan and the three WSL lanes; the real binary on this machine's GPU wrote a startup hitch capture, served `diag.capabilities`, refused a bad policy and, armed, lost its device at the planned submission with the injected-fault capture written; 9 teeth shown. **All local clauses are shown**; the row waits only for its first hosted run (Needs CI once a published run carries this work), which must show crd-perf-tests with the `[diag9b]`, `[fault]`, `[assets]`, `[doctor]` (with `external-io-replay`) and `[identity]` cases, crd-ceir-cook-tests (with the two `[diag9b]` external I/O cases), crd-platform-tests, crd-resources-tests (with the `[cook]`, `[install]` and `[teardown]` cases), crd-scene-render-tests (with the `[install]` case), crd-cooker-tests, crd-ceridc-tests, crd-sandbox-inspect-tests and the 12-input bundle fuzz replay green on all six lanes, crd-sandbox building on every lane, the Windows minidump and freed-DLL cases on the hosted Windows runners and the `/proc/self/maps` case on the hosted Linux runners; the DX12 `[diag9b]` device-loss case run, not skipped, on WARP on the hosted Windows lanes and the Vulkan one on lavapipe on the Linux lanes. The real-binary runs are local evidence only: no hosted job runs crd-sandbox. 2026-10-11 (from DIAG.10b, [session](sessions/2026-10-11-diag-10b-engine-seams.md)): the fatal path that finds the ring held (`ring_unavailable`, FatalEntered) and the flight reload generation wrap now have `[diag9b][diag10b]` cases in crd-perf-tests through test-only seams; the hosted run must show them green on every lane with asserts. 2026-10-12 (from DIAG.11a, [session](sessions/2026-10-12-diag-11a-reusable-diagnostic-host.md)): the sandbox's flight host became the engine's `DiagnosticHost` and its seven `[flight-host]` cases moved to crd-perf-tests as `[diag9b][diag11a][diag-host]` cases started from a profile; the hosted run must show them there (no device needed: the injection branches on every lane with fault seams; on the shipping lanes fault.* is not served, so those branches check UnknownCommand). **Done (2026-10-12):** the `[diag9b]`, `[fault]`, `[assets]`, `[doctor]` and `[identity]` cases, the 15 `[diag-host]` cases and the `[diag10b]` cases (7 on lanes with asserts, 4 on win-shipping) are green on all six lanes in complete run [38236026326](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/38236026326) (push) at `7a958b88`; the DX12 device-loss case runs on WARP with 48 assertions (6 on win-shipping), the Vulkan one on lavapipe with 52, the minidump and freed-DLL cases pass on the Windows lanes, the build-id `/proc/self/maps` case on the Linux lanes, and the bundle corpus replays its 12 inputs everywhere.
