| 095 | <a id="slice-hw-resilience.1"></a>**HW-RESILIENCE.1** | Open | Data integrity on faulty hardware (user decision 2026-10-26: even faulty hardware must work gracefully). Every cooked asset, compressed block, network packet and saved document carries a checksum verified after read and after decompression; a mismatch retries once from the source, then fails with a typed error and a clear message, never a crash or silent bad data. Saves are atomic with checksums and keep previous generations, with autosave and journaling so a crash costs seconds of work. Adversarial tests flip bits in each path and prove detection, retry and recovery. | [RESEARCH-ADOPT](#slice-research-adopt) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Decisions](sessions/2026-10-26-user-decisions-platform-floors-and-research.md); [Evidence](sessions/2026-10-26-catch2-fault-address.md) |
| 096 | <a id="slice-hw-resilience.2"></a>**HW-RESILIENCE.2** | Open | Hardware identity and fault classification. Crash and failure bundles record CPU model, microcode, BIOS, GPU and driver and memory configuration; bundle.inspect classifies "impossible state" signatures (values no store wrote, faults in code that cannot fault, repeats that do not replay) as likely hardware faults with their evidence; the crash service (OPS.1) clusters reports by CPU model and microcode. A known-unstable CPU/microcode table and repeated hardware signatures produce a plain, kind user message that names the likely cause and the fix (for example a BIOS update), never blame without evidence. | [HW-RESILIENCE.1](#slice-hw-resilience.1) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Decisions](sessions/2026-10-26-user-decisions-platform-floors-and-research.md); [Evidence](sessions/2026-10-26-catch2-fault-address.md) |
| 097 | <a id="slice-hw-resilience.3"></a>**HW-RESILIENCE.3** | Open | Self-checks and graceful degradation. At startup the engine runs fast known-answer tests for every dispatched kernel level (SIMD-DISPATCH) and device path; a path that fails falls back to a safer level or path, keeps running and reports why. `ceridc doctor --hardware` runs the Cerid-free control stress check (the 2026-10-26 host check) and reports pass/fail per core and arm so users and support can test a machine. Deterministic simulations and lockstep sessions compare state hashes, detect divergence quickly and name the diverging machine. | [HW-RESILIENCE.2](#slice-hw-resilience.2) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Decisions](sessions/2026-10-26-user-decisions-platform-floors-and-research.md); [Evidence](sessions/2026-10-26-catch2-fault-address.md) |
| 098 | <a id="slice-hw-resilience.4"></a>**HW-RESILIENCE.4** | Open | Everything explained: a user guide (what Cerid does on unstable hardware, what each message means, how to test and fix a machine), a support/agent runbook in the diagnostic runbooks, the design reference and ADR note, and the MCP surface for agents to run the checks and read the classification. Qualified with injected faults on every lane and the hosted host-check arms. | [HW-RESILIENCE.3](#slice-hw-resilience.3) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Decisions](sessions/2026-10-26-user-decisions-platform-floors-and-research.md); [Evidence](sessions/2026-10-26-catch2-fault-address.md) |
| 099 | <a id="slice-hw-resilience"></a>**HW-RESILIENCE** | Open | Hardware-fault resilience closed: all children pass, faulty hardware degrades gracefully with no lost work and every behaviour documented. | [HW-RESILIENCE.4](#slice-hw-resilience.4) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Decisions](sessions/2026-10-26-user-decisions-platform-floors-and-research.md); [Evidence](sessions/2026-10-26-catch2-fault-address.md) |
| 100 | <a id="slice-diag.11b"></a>**DIAG.11b** | Partial | Measured disabled/basic/full overhead, focused local routes, tiered sanitizer/race/fuzz CI and investigation runbooks; DG18. **Overhead harness, event-site form and first boards landed locally** (2026-10-16, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-16-diag-11b-overhead-harness.md), [board](bench/2026-10-16-diag-11b-overhead.md)): `CRD_FLIGHT_RECORD` evaluates a site's payload only when a recorder runs and its detail keeps the event (`FlightRecorder::admit`), and `record()` drops a left-out event before the clock or the ring mutex; side-effect and no-wait cases (`[diag11b]`) with teeth. `crd-diag-overhead` (`tests/support/diag-overhead`) measures the workloads alloc, jobs, offline and events in the modes off, basic and full, one process each, with the authored `overhead_basic`/`overhead_full` flight policies and `overhead_offline.ceir`; its 12 `crd-diag-overhead-*` CTests hold the structural half on every lane (off evaluates no payload and starts no diagnostic thread, basic filters every event, full records every one, every capture delivered, the lifetime ledger balanced). `scripts/diag-overhead.py` interleaves processes and judges ADR-0133 ID-5 (within, over, or unresolved as a loss; `test-diag-overhead.py` covers it; ID-5 amended with the workload deviations). Boards on win-shipping-profile against win-shipping, linux-gcc-shipping and linux-gcc-relwithdebinfo: storage within everywhere. **The installed application's sites** (2026-10-16, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-16-diag-11b-installed-sites.md)): `diag-consumer` writes every event site with `CRD_FLIGHT_RECORD` and formats a message only inside it (`site_text`, counted); `crd-package-consumer` runs the failure at the full and the basic detail and holds `CONSUMER_SITES` and the bundle to the kept events (40 of 40 formatted and recorded at full; 5 of 40 at basic, the 35 informational orders left out unformatted, the bundle holding the five refunds); an eager site fails the basic arm (formatted 40, expected 5). **The shipping lane's link-time warnings** (2026-10-17, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-17-diag-11b-link-time-warnings.md)): the hosted linux-gcc-shipping lane's 44 GCC LTO diagnostics (13 sites) and 35 `lto-wrapper` lines are repaired with nothing suppressed: `swap_last2` bounded by `kMaxRank`, `pop_back` trims in the ceridc diag tests, a non-negative node count in `eval_cpu`, closed-form ends of `pack_b`'s interleave and `convert_f32_to_f16`'s vector part, the heterogeneous `CommandRegistry::find`, and for the two DIAG false positives a clear-then-copy `set_path` and `String::append`'s heap contract after `reserve`; the zstd IPO guard names the real `libzstd_static` (it never applied) and zstd's unused legacy decoders are off. A WSL relink of all 189 executables of the configuration prints no warning (before: all 44 reproduced). **Warnings held at zero** (2026-10-17, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-17-diag-11b-warning-gates.md)): GCC and Clang link lines carry `-Werror` under the same switch as the compile lines (`crd-warnings`' link options; probes showed GCC judges link-time optimization's diagnostics by the link line alone and `lto-wrapper`'s notices ignore it), and full builds of linux-gcc-release, linux-gcc-relwithdebinfo and linux-gcc-shipping link every executable under it with no warning (a restored `set_path` false positive fails the link); crd-jobs' MSVC `/Od` pin compiles in `engine/foundation/jobs/unoptimized/` without the configuration's `/O2`, so win-shipping, win-shipping-profile and win-release no longer print D9025, with the pinned code unchanged (disassembly and symbols of all 14 pinned objects on four lanes), and `crd-check-compile-commands` fails any cl.exe/clang-cl command carrying two optimization levels on every Windows Ninja lane (teeth: the strip turned off fails it). win-clang-cl-shipping's two zstd `-Wunused-function` were left for their own batch (below). **The warning set at the link** (2026-10-17, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-17-diag-11b-link-time-warning-set.md)): probes showed GCC's link-time compiler takes no warning option from the objects and `lto-wrapper` forwards no C-family option, `-Wall` included, so a `-Wall`-class defect exposed only by cross-file inlining linked green; the GCC link line now names the compile set's options lto1 reads (`-Wextra -Wpedantic -Wshadow` and `-Wall`'s members, `-Warray-bounds=1` and `-Wmaybe-uninitialized` among them; `-Wrestrict`, `-Wdangling-pointer`, `-Wmismatched-new-delete` and the format-overflow class cannot reach it on GCC 13.3), and GLFW builds without IPO on GCC so its code never meets them. The 14 sites the set found on the three IPO trees are repaired, none suppressed: real ones in the CKIR scan builders (a per-thread chunk outside 1..64 read an unset register or wrote past it, now refused), einsum's plan (no step count after saturated greedy sweeps), `HashMap::operator[]` (an unset index on a corrupt table, now fatal) and a test artifact's unset member; the rest restated so the bounds are visible. A cross-file out-of-bounds read links green under the old line and fails the link under the set (teeth). `-Wnull-dereference` reached lto1 too and was left for its own batch. **Link-time null dereferences** (2026-10-17, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-17-diag-11b-link-time-null-dereference.md)): the GCC link line now carries `-Wnull-dereference`, and its 80 link-time locations (six collection rounds on linux-gcc-shipping, the stubborn links relinked with `-flto-partition=one` to list every site) and the 42 more linux-gcc-relwithdebinfo shows with asserts compiled in (an ignorable assert keeps the null arm) are repaired, none suppressed. Lookups an invariant makes certain, which followed a null after an assert that compiles out, gained invariant accessors fatal in every build with no added compare on the hot path (`ComponentRegistry::require_info`, `ArchetypeGraph::require`, `HashMap::at`, `toml::node::child_at`, `func::func_body`; probe disassembly: the same 11 branches in each scene accessor). Allocation refusals are the named out-of-memory fatal through `construct.hpp`'s new `*_or_fatal` helpers: the CEIR context's IR arena, and the containers, which adopted a null buffer (a refused `Array` growth was relocated into null and kept its capacity; `String`, `HashMap`, `RingBuffer`, `SpscQueue`, `AtomicArray`, `ConcurrentQueue` likewise), and `allocate_array` refuses a byte size above `PTRDIFF_MAX` (the release tree's one new link-time warning, a memset bound GCC derived from the checked size in `dlasd1`, which now makes dlasd1.f's argument checks). Real defects: `TransformPropagation` dereferenced a `ChildOf` child without a `Transform` and never recomputed a dirty leaf under a dirty organisation node; the transform writers wrote through null on a dead entity or a missing `Transform`; `Commands::add_component` of an unregistered type read a null `ComponentInfo`; the renderer read a resource handle's payload twice; `TlsfAllocator`'s borrowing constructor wrote its sentinels through a null buffer; the authored rewrite driver's build action followed a missing definer; `smoke_mesh`'s checks could never fail a release run; test oracles dereferenced nullable lookups. A null lineage map handed to `expand_ml_ops`' driver made IPA-CP specialise `HashMap::rehash` for a null `this` (the link's IPA-CP dump names the call); the driver takes a recorder callable. The crash specimens' deliberate null writes go through a volatile pointer (GCC had rewritten them into its own trap). Tests with teeth: three `[organisation]` transform cases; contract-fatal CTests in child processes (`crd_diag_fatal_tests`: `crd-containers-fatal-*` 9, `crd-scene-fatal-*` 6, `crd-ceir-fatal-*` 5, `crd-toml-fatal-*` 1, and five alloc-fatal specimen modes for the helpers and the borrowed buffer), each failing or crashing with its check removed; reverting one repair fails the shipping link under the new line and links green under the old. **zstd's clang-cl and configure warnings; where a pause lands** (2026-10-17, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-17-diag-11b-zstd-configure-and-pause-landing.md)): a sweep of every hosted job log of run 38640651640 found, besides the warnings repaired above, zstd 1.5.5's configure deprecation on all 21 build jobs, CMP0194 on the 10 MSVC jobs, two unused `_NOTE_*` preset variables and win-clang-cl-shipping's two zstd `-Wunused-function`; all reproduced locally from fresh configures. No zstd release (v1.5.7, dev) changes the `__GNUC__` gate on `MEM_STATIC`'s unused attribute, so two registered patches on build-owned copies (`cmake/patches/zstd-*-unused.cmake`, swapped into `libzstd_static`'s sources) give the two assert-only helpers the attribute under clang-cl only; the objects are the upstream objects (identical code on win-clang-cl-shipping, win-shipping-profile and linux-gcc-shipping; with asserts only the asserts' line numbers move). `CMAKE_POLICY_VERSION_MINIMUM` 3.10 ends the deprecation on CMake 4 (zstd sets `cmake_policy(VERSION 3.13.0)` itself), CMP0194 is answered `OLD` for zstd's directory under both its names (CMake 4.1 to 4.3 read the default as `CMP194`, probed), and the preset notes became `description`s; the three fresh configures and every lane's zstd build then print nothing. The DIAG.8b pause case's oracle expected `NotYetComputed` for whatever op a pause held, but a request lands at the next safe point before any instr in any frame, and `core.for` defines no result (`NoSuchValue`), where a slow TSan thread had landed: the case asserts the landing rule, and a new case pins landings on `core.for`, the loop body and the callee through the session's observer (teeth: the old oracle, an executor that skips resultless instrs, a `read_value` that counts the held instr as run; 100 TSan repeats clean). The edit exposed one link-time `-Wnull-dereference` in `test_hello.cpp` (an unchecked `func_body_block`), repaired with `func::func_body`. **Allocation refusals in the scene and the resource manager** (2026-10-17, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-17-diag-11b-unchecked-allocations.md)): follow-up (h) and the same shape swept through the scene, resources and memory modules. A sub-budget allocator refuses by returning nullptr, and these sites wrote through the result behind an assert a build without asserts drops: a relation's reverse index (`World::on_relation_registered`), a sparse-set pool's dense array (`grow_dense`, also an unchecked size) and its lazily created `SharedComponentPool`, that pool's entry array (`SharedComponentPool::grow`), the resource manager's control blocks (`make_failed_block` and the first block of `load_sync`, `load_async` and `load_streamed`, now one `new_control_block`) and its async and streamed load contexts, and the borrowing `LinearAllocator`, `StackAllocator` and `PoolAllocator` constructors handed a null buffer (the borrowing pool also gets the owning constructor's checked span). Each is now a named out-of-memory fatal in every build through `construct_or_fatal`/`allocate_or_fatal`; `ArchetypeGraph::archetype_for` and `ChunkAllocator::allocate`, already stopped by their pool's generic fatal, take `try_allocate` and name themselves. Tests with teeth: six more `crd-scene-fatal-*` modes, a new `crd-resources-fatal-specimen` with six modes that refuse by request shape (a control block is the only request of its size and alignment), and four borrowed modes in crd-memory-tests' arena case; with the nine sources set back to HEAD every new mode fails (the old assert, the pool's generic fatal, or a crash on the refused pointer). The other unchecked sites in the engine are queued as (m). **Scene and öbek packs at the trust boundary** (2026-10-17, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-17-diag-11b-scene-pack-trust.md)): follow-up (i) and, from (m), the scene module's shared pool. `SceneLoader` and `ObekLoader` refuse a pack whose records do not lie inside it (shared rules in `engine/world/scene/src/pack_validation.hpp`: at most `kMaxComponents` descriptors, each with a unique non-zero FourCC and a non-zero size that is a multiple of its power-of-two alignment; payload chunks holding exactly their records with checked arithmetic, so the `u32` payload product no longer wraps, and entity indices strictly ascending below the entity count; relation records in range, ascending by source, one per source and relation; OBEK entity, override and chain records in range) and copy every record table into typed storage (a compressed chunk can decode misaligned; UBSan flags the old in-place view). `World::instantiate_scene`/`instantiate_obek` refuse an incompatible pack before the first spawn with a `PackRefusal` and the deciding FourCC (malformed views, a size, alignment or version mismatch, a cycle in an Acyclic relation), the contract ADR-0055 §5 sets (amended in place with ADR-0058 §9; the `world.hpp` comment called it `CRD_FATAL` while the code asserted). Five more defects found reading the paths and repaired: `SceneLoader` kept views into bytes the resource manager frees when `load()` returns (every scene loaded through the manager read freed memory); relation components' payload records were restored as source-World ids before the relation re-add, which in a fresh World saw the same id and skipped the reverse-index edge (destroying a loaded parent did not cascade); records were staged in a 1024-byte stack buffer at 16-byte alignment (now in place only when they fit, else one block at the type's alignment, a named fatal when refused); cook-time and caller patches and `revert_field` wrote relation targets past the reverse index, with wrapping `u32` offset sums; the batch counter was a process-wide `static`. `SharedComponentPool::grown_capacity` doubles in `u64` and clamps to the index range (fatal past it) and `incremented_refcount` is fatal past `u32`. Tests with teeth: `test_scene_pack_trust.cpp` (14 cases: 25 corruptions of SCEN and OBEK packs and 8 of the OBEK-only tables, each breaking one rule, with the 19 new rules' 38 cases failing when the new loader checks are removed and the six older rules still refusing; the instantiation refusals; a scene loaded through a real `ResourceManager`; the reverse index in a fresh World; patches and reverts on `ChildOf`; a 1536-byte 64-aligned component; the capacity helper), and three more `crd-scene-fatal-*` modes (`staging-refused`, `shared-index-exhausted`, `shared-refcount-overflow`). **Body lookups held to their check** (2026-10-17, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-17-diag-11b-func-body-lookups.md)): follow-up (k). 213 dereferenced `func::func_body_block` lookups in 63 files (62 test files across crd-ceir, -ceir-cook, -ceir-gpu, -ceir-gpu-dx12/-vulkan/-cuda, -ceir-host, -chir, -audio and -frame-cook tests, and `ChirLowerer`'s handler body in `chir/src/lower.cpp`, which four lowerings dereferenced) take `func::func_body`, fatal in every build; the 31 helpers keep their `Block*` return. The `create_func` results they come from are `REQUIRE`d (107 definitions, three of them in the previous batch's `test_hello.cpp` and `test_inspect.cpp`, two `ModuleBuilder::func`, three `mkfunc` helpers; `CRD_FATAL` in the Catch2-free `ceir_render_triangle.hpp`). The four test sites that test the lookup and the seven engine sites that branch on it keep it. `crd-ceir-invariants` gains I7: every `func_body_block` call in engine, tests, tools, runtime and sandbox tests its result against nullptr on its line or through its variable within six lines (teeth: an assigned, a returned and a chained unchecked lookup and a removed `REQUIRE` each fail both scripts, under gawk, mawk and PowerShell 5.1). The CEIR-17a scene-dialect cases (`tests/execution/ceir/test_scene.cpp`) were in no target's source list and had never run; crd-ceir-tests builds them now (a blind `find_scene_misuse` fails the rejection case). **Allocation refusals in the rest of the engine** (2026-10-18, committed, not pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-18-diag-11b-engine-allocation-refusals.md)): follow-up (m). The 66 queued sites and six more a fresh sweep found (the optimization model's three functions, the scene renderer's staged frame graph, the scene and öbek cookers' registries) wrote through a refused allocation, several sized by an unchecked product. Each module keeps its convention: hesap-tensor's sparse scratch its `AllocFailed` status (a byte size that overflows is now refused as well), every other one a named out-of-memory fatal in every build through `*_or_fatal`: the CEIR reload set (cook Context, generation, a generation's Context), the plan cache's copies, the host provider's pooled-launch token, the CEIR diagnostic engine's arena copies, the CKIR reference interpreter's and reverse AD's scratch, hesap-fft (`FftPlan`'s 30 tables through one `plan_table`, its four-step sub-plans, the Bluestein, N-d and sparse plans' inner plans), hesap-dense (`Matrix` with a checked element count, `Vector`, `Permutation`, structured storage with checked sizes, the GEMM, small GEMM, parallel GEMM and SYRK packing buffers), the DSP and CWT plans, the model's functions, the renderer and the cookers; `allocate_array`/`allocate_array_or_fatal` take an explicit alignment so the 64- and 32-byte tables keep theirs. Two defects found on the way: an Attention node's row of scores was never returned to the scratch (a heap scratch leaked one per evaluation), and `VTape::make` checked its node capacity only in an assert, so a build without asserts wrote past both arrays (now fatal in every build; the pool size is checked before anything is allocated). Tests with teeth: 44 new child-process modes on a shared `refusing_allocator.hpp` that refuses by rule (all, one request shape, an alignment, after N grants) so each mode's allocation is its run's first refusal (`crd-ceir-cook-fatal-*` 5, `crd-ceir-host-fatal-*` 1, `crd-ceir-fatal-diagnostic-*` 3, `crd-kir-fatal-*` 3, `crd-hesap-fft-fatal-*` 8, `crd-hesap-dense-fatal-*` 10, `crd-hesap-dsp-fatal-*` 3, `crd-hesap-wavelet-fatal-*` 1, `crd-hesap-autodiff-fatal-*` 4, `crd-hesap-opt-fatal-*` 3, `crd-scene-cooker-fatal-*` 2, `crd-scene-render-fatal-*` 1), two more alloc-fatal modes (the aligned form, an overflowing count) and unit cases for the aligned form, the attention scratch balance and the sparse overflow; with the four helpers' fatal removed all 40 refusal modes fail (30 crash on the refused pointer, 10 return without the fatal), and with the explicit checks removed the four overflow and capacity modes and the three unit cases fail. `check-allocation-results.py` (`crd-check-allocation-results` on every lane, four unit cases) fails a raw allocation result in engine, tools, runtime or sandbox code that is used without a nullptr check, a status, a pass-up or a named heap site (10 files, 17 counted calls); on HEAD's sources it reports the 71 sites this batch repaired, on the tree none of 58 calls. Strict tidy on the edited headers brought their older findings into the gate and they are repaired (216 in fft.hpp, mostly declarations split one per statement, and 24 in the dense, DSP, CWT, sparse and model headers; `Matrix::layout`, `MatrixView::layout` and `Triangular::side`/`diag` are now `kLayout`, `kSide`, `kDiag`). **Faster local builds, task A** (2026-10-18, pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-18-diag-11b-build-speed-cache-mirror.md), [board](bench/2026-10-18-build-speed-cache-mirror.md)): the user's extra batch. The local helpers build through the pinned sccache 0.17.0 (`configure-preset.bat`, `build-target.bat`, `compiler-cache.py`: cl and clang-cl without precompiled headers, Shipping `/Z7` only under the launcher, `-Xclang -fno-pch-timestamp` on Clang; the presets and the default graph unchanged); the Linux lanes build and test from the ext4 mirror `~/cerid-src` (`source-mirror.py`, `wsl-lane.sh`: synced before every configure and build, a stale mirror refused before tests, one heavy job at a time, the pinned validation layer exported); long commands wait through `wait-for.py`, bounded under the loop watchdog; and 14 hosted lanes cache through sccache's GitHub Actions backend in per-lane namespaces, with `--show-stats` in the log and the evidence bundle (`check-ci-tiers.py` holds the list; the MSVC LTCG and cold-acquisition lanes stay uncached). Measured: the loop's one-tooth cycle across the seven local lanes 549.1 → 108.6 s (79.3 s repeated), linux-gcc-debug cold 1,131.9 → 178.7 s and its teeth restore 1,033.6 → 25.2 s, the win-debug teeth restore 141.1 → 37.0 s; the losses (a cold miss +12 to +51 %, MSVC LTCG only -17 %) are in the board. cdb resolves frames, lines and locals in programs built from the cache; eight helper mutations each fail their tests. **Faster local builds, task B** (2026-10-18, pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-18-diag-11b-build-speed-linking.md), [board](bench/2026-10-18-build-speed-linking.md)): the pinned mold 3.0.0 (`tools/mold-linux`, `install-mold.py`) and the module's opt-in `CRD_LINKER`/`CRD_LINKER_THREADS`; the helpers (`compiler-cache.py`, `wsl-lane.sh`) link linux-gcc-debug, linux-gcc-asan and linux-clang-tsan with it at CPUs / lane jobs threads per link (two here), the three mirror trees converted; presets, the default graph and every hosted lane unchanged. Measured: mold's default pool (a thread per CPU per link) gained nothing at 16 jobs (clang-tsan teeth +29 %, gcc-asan +9 %); bounded, a whole-tree relink's summed link time fell 106.3 → 54.8 s (gcc-debug), 179.2 → 81.9 s (gcc-asan) and 125.5 → 25.6 s (clang-tsan), its wall time by 23-48 %, and a one-program relink of crd-ceir-tests takes 0.7-0.9 s against 1.1-1.5 s; suites, gdb and the gdb diag CTests are identical on both linkers, no build prints a linker warning, and the link-time warning tooth fails the LTO link under mold and under GNU ld. Not kept, by measurement: mold on the GCC LTO presets (7.2-7.5 s against 7.3-8.0 s), `-gsplit-dwarf` (GCC refuses the precompiled header under it; against the PCH-off tree 1.2 s of a 9.1 s warm relink), and on win-debug `/INCREMENTAL:NO` (doubles the link time) and `/DEBUG:FASTLINK` (ties the default `/INCREMENTAL` on edits, doubles a full relink). A fault the boards found is repaired: WSL2 relays a Linux localhost port to Windows, so a lane build's sccache server on the shared default port answered the Windows clients and blocked a Windows server, and the helpers hid the failed start; the Linux server now has its own port (4227), the server step proves a listening server on its cache or fails, and both helpers stop the build on that failure (18 helper mutations each fail their tests). **Faster local builds, task C, part 1: loose ends and the profile route** (2026-10-18, pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-18-diag-11b-build-speed-compile-time.md)): nightly run 39013990584's five DX12 ray-tracing failures on win-asan are [TP-1](third-party-defects.md#tp-1) (the register's WARP read, pc and region sizes; the gate named each `registered TP-1: failed`, and the next two runs pass the gate with exactly those five). Clause (8) is not met by the user's elevated run of 2026-10-07, which sampled DIAG.6c's hotspot specimen; it is now part of the user's one elevated command, `scripts\elevated-captures.bat` (`sample-overhead-wpr.py record --progress`): every arm (compiled-out, off, basic, full) of the alloc, jobs and offline workloads runs as a process of one WPR trace, and `analyze` (no elevation) attributes each process's CPU samples to the diagnostic frames, reports CPU milliseconds per frame, and judges whether each mode's measured cost is explained by them (`test-sample-overhead-wpr.py`, 12 cases, a CTest and a repository step; seven mutations each fail it; unelevated, `record` reports `PERMISSION_DENIED` and leaves nothing). Boards record the host's memory and busiest processes before and after, the Windows host's too on WSL, and refuse a contended host (`build-bench.py host`; four mutations each fail its case). `build-profile.py` profiles a preset's compile time in a fresh uncached tree (MSVC `/Bt+ /d1reportTime`; Clang `-ftime-trace` read by the pinned ClangBuildAnalyzer 1.6.0, `install-clang-build-analyzer.py`, built from source into `~/.cerid/tools`); C++ Build Insights' `vcperf` is installed but needs an elevated shell. **Faster local builds, task C, part 2: compile time** (2026-10-18, pushed; [session](sessions/2026-10-18-diag-11b-build-speed-compile-time.md#the-reductions), [board](bench/2026-10-18-build-speed-compile-time.md)): profiles of uncached win-debug and Clang builds (contended host, so they rank) put a third of the front end in standard headers that hot headers pulled into every unit, `<chrono>` first, through Catch2's assertion path. Two registered Catch2 patches (`catch2-chrono-stringmaker-opt-in`, `catch2-clock-chrono-subset`; failure messages of durations are the one visible change) and the unused includes of `array.hpp` (`<iterator>`), `hash.hpp` and `hash_map.hpp` (`<functional>`; the containers' default key equality is `DefaultEqual`) and `jobs.hpp` (`<algorithm>`), with the scalar exp and log cores apart from their AVX2 twins (`crd/math/exp_log_core.hpp`; identical disassembly on GCC, Clang and MSVC): `<chrono>` reaches 240 of 1,832 MSVC units and 224 Clang units instead of 1,155 and 1,126, and the Clang front end falls 3,095 → 2,110 s, its build 233 → 168 s at the same load. Whole-tree keep-going builds (win-debug, linux-gcc-debug, linux-clang-tsan; `wsl-lane.sh` gained `CRD_LANE_KEEP_GOING=1`) exposed eight missing includes, added in the consumers; strict tidy repaired the touched headers' older findings. `check-include-budget.py` (`crd-check-include-budget`, `include-budget.json`) fails a hot header that reaches a forbidden standard header again, with the include chain (teeth for every cut on MSVC and GCC). no loss beyond one 53 s configure and one `LNK1103` rerun. `crd/math/deterministic.hpp` declares its two vector types instead of including them (`simd/backend.hpp` 584 → 418 Clang units, `<immintrin.h>` 602 → 456), held by two more include-budget entries, and the budget's probes and strict tidy strip Clang's `-Xclang -include-pch` spelling as well (a Clang probe had listed none of the precompiled header's headers). `dev.py plan` prints each risk gate's route (`risk_routes`: local commands, notes and hosted lanes, the `--path` scenario carried; Catch2's library compiles as unity batches of 16 (`CRD_CATCH2_UNITY`, third-party only; summed compile 106-113 → 9 s on MSVC, 69-77 → 10-11 s on GCC and Clang, the same global symbols and suite counts), and its own units reach the patched Catch2 copies first as well (they had compiled the unpatched headers: a plain `-I` outranks a system directory), held by a ninth include-budget entry. Nineteen entries entered from the symptom (which tests and lanes, a red hosted lane, a crash, a fatal invariant, a hang, a race, a bad memory access, a stale handle, a leak, a GPU error or loss, a failing authored program, an exact replay, a rare failure, a slow frame, a hostile input, a silent detector, a wrong number or pressured resource, switching the diagnostics on, a build gate), each a table of symptom, command, artifact, read, next discriminating test, hosted lanes, the one adoption step, a working example, the cost and the owning children; every child owns one except DIAG.11c and DIAG.12, listed with their reasons; human-only publication stated (ADR-0133 ID-6). `check-diag-runbooks.py` (`crd-check-diag-runbooks` on every Ninja lane, with that lane's tree) fails a script, flag, lane command, preset, `ceridc` verb, diag command, grant, executable, binary directory or CMake name that does not exist, a test regex matching no test of the tree on the rows for its host, an artifact the sources never write, a missing example, a cost without its link, an uncovered child, and any command that commits, pushes, releases, opens an issue or pull request, dispatches a workflow, writes through `gh api` or grants `upload` or `remote-enable`; 21 document breaks and five guard mutations each fail its cases, and a dead regex fails the CTest. Read against every hosted lane's test list (complete run 39174216672), three references matched nothing where a mode is compiled out (the RT sentinels without asserts, the CPROF and Perfetto cases without profiling): rows now take build qualifiers, `(asserts)` and `(profiling)`, read from the tree's `CMakeCache.txt`, and all 20 lanes' lists pass. **Every source held to a build route** (2026-10-19; [session](sessions/2026-10-19-diag-11b-source-routes.md), [contract](design/test-instruments.md#source-routes)): follow-up (n). `check-source-routes.py` (`crd-check-source-routes` on every full configuration with a compile database) judges every first-party C-family source under engine, tests, tools, runtime, sandbox, bench and scripts, tracked or untracked, against what the configured tree compiles (its `compile_commands.json`) and `scripts/source-routes.json`: a source no entry names is compiled by a target; a `built_when` entry's conditions, read from the tree (the platform, a `CMakeCache.txt` option a CMake file defines, a target that compiles sources: the D3D12, CUDA, WebGPU, HIP and Metal backends, the fiber-switch assembly, the benchmark suites, the peer-bench options), hold exactly when its sources are compiled; `built_by` and `included_by` files name their sources (the package consumer's own project, the GenMC model's checker, the peer scripts, a generated kernel's includer); `unrouted` needs an open ROADMAP owner and is refused in engine, tests, tools and sandbox. The 32 peer-free retained programs of `runtime/examples` build under `CRD_BUILD_RETAINED_BENCHES`, on in linux-gcc-debug and the presets inheriting it (the runtime module declares the six hesap modules they link); building them found what had rotted, all repaired with nothing disabled: seven discarded `[[nodiscard]]` build statuses, deprecated `getenv`/`fopen` and POSIX `clock_gettime` (MSVC), nine implicit conversions, a zero-length format and two shadowed locals (GCC), and 18 strict-tidy findings in the nine touched files. The two geometry-convex probes are removed as their own headers asked (the EPA one is `test_epa.cpp`'s rotated hull-vs-hull case). 37 peer or vendor programs that no script or option builds (all of `bench/` but its generated kernels, two `scripts/` programs, ten Boost, MKL, SUNDIALS and liquid-dsp benches and the three NLopt difftests of `runtime/examples`) are unrouted, owned by [REPO.3](#slice-repo.3). Teeth: dropping `test_scene.cpp` from crd-ceir-tests again fails the CTest after a reconfigure, an untracked test source fails it without one, and fifteen mutations of the guard each fail its twelve cases. **Allocator statistics on request** (2026-10-19; [session](sessions/2026-10-19-diag-11b-allocator-statistics-on-request.md), [board](bench/2026-10-19-diag-11b-allocator-statistics.md)): clause (1). A profiling build without `CRD_DEBUG` (win-shipping-profile) counts an allocator only once a reader requests it before the allocator's first allocation (`IAllocator::request_statistics()`, which `crd::perf::register_allocator` calls; a per-allocator `StatisticsGate`, one inlined load and branch per operation, the first allocation's exchange and the counting out of line); a `CRD_DEBUG` build counts every allocator from construction as before. `MemoryStats::coverage()` (`crd/memory/statistics_coverage.hpp`) names what the counts cover (complete, not requested, too late, elsewhere for the decorators and composites, compiled out), carried by the profiler's snapshots, every `AllocatorRecord` (`_pad` renamed `stamps`, the coverage in bits 32-39, a value this build does not know read as unrecorded), `memory.allocators`' `statistics` field and the panel's Counted column, so a zero is never read as a measurement; a moved `GrowablePoolAllocator` takes its source's counts (the destination's frees had wrapped `bytes_in_use`). Tests with teeth: `test_statistics_coverage.cpp` (the gate's rules and a request racing the first allocations in every build, each build's own answer on real allocators, the move), the profiler's late-registration, decorator and record-stamp cases, the allocator fuzz target requesting every allocator's statistics, and the overhead harness holding its heap to what the build counts unasked in every mode; the gate removed fails the harness's three alloc modes and the coverage cases on win-shipping-profile, registration without a request the snapshot cases there, and a granted late request, an allocation that loses the first exchange left uncounted, the move without its counts, an unchecked capture value and an unstamped record each fail their cases. Measured: the allocation-heavy disabled cost fell +37.7% → +4.7% to +5.0%, still over; a gate-against-no-gate pair attributes it to the gate itself (no gate: +0.12%), and offline moved -11.0% against compiled-out between two builds that do not touch its path, a layout floor of the two-binary method. Found on the way, queued as (p): `MallocAllocator`'s byte statistics never balance (Windows: `allocation_size` is 0 for an `_aligned_malloc` block, so a free never lowers `bytes_in_use`: 1,100 bytes allocated and freed leave 1,100; glibc: the request is counted at allocation and the usable size at free, so the same pair wraps it to 2^64 - 52). **The default allocator's exact sizes** (2026-10-19; [session](sessions/2026-10-19-diag-11b-malloc-allocator-bytes.md)): follow-up (p). `MallocAllocator` aligns inside one C-library `malloc` and keeps a 16-byte header below each block (the request, and the offset to the C-library block with log2 of the alignment), so `allocation_size()` is the request on every platform and its statistics count the same bytes at allocation and at free; `smoke_memory`'s default heap read 789,028 bytes in use, equal to its peak, after 17 frees, and now 787,520 under a peak of 787,779. Under ASan the header and the alignment padding are poisoned and read through the new `CRD_MEM_ASAN_UNCHECKED` loads (`crd/memory/asan_poison.hpp`), so a run before or past a block is still reported; a header that fails its check (an alignment the pointer lacks, an offset outside its budget, a zero size) is a named fatal at the free in every build, never a `free()` of a computed pointer (a corruption check, not ownership: `owns()` stays true). Tests with teeth: the exact size at alignments 1 to 4,096 in every build, the bytes back at their baseline after every free (requested before the first allocation, so exact on the `CRD_DEBUG` lanes and win-shipping-profile), the debug statistics case made exact, the ASan layout case, the negative control's `malloc-underrun` mode (use-after-poison, exit 42) and `crd-diag-alloc-fatal-specimen`'s `malloc-header-clobbered` mode; with HEAD's allocator source they fail on win-debug (sizes 0, bytes stuck at 6,100, the free crashing in `_aligned_free`), win-asan (the sizes, `malloc-underrun` exit 43) and linux-gcc-debug (sizes `24 == 1`, the bytes wrapping to 2^64 - 3,252, glibc aborting in `free()`). Also repaired: `msvc-env.bat` puts the Visual Studio installer directory on `PATH`, since VsDevCmd runs `vswhere.exe` by bare name and a shell with `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath` printed "'vswhere.exe' is not recognized" and left `VSCMD_VER` unset. Found on the way and repaired, a tier hole: the push group replaces a pending run, and the replacing run classified its own `before..HEAD`, so a replaced push's changes never entered a tier decision (39216534845 at `86f4b900` started no job and its replacement classified only `86f4b900..59807aab`; every replacing run since `b5e9a003` happened to resolve complete, so no coverage was lost). `ci-tier.py base` now gives a push the head of the newest earlier run whose `Preflight (tier resolution)` succeeded (the preflight reads the run list with `actions: read`; no answer is the complete tier, never `before`; [contract](design/ci-tiers.md#resolution)); two repository cases and five mutations hold it. **The disabled mode on instructions** (2026-10-24, [session](sessions/2026-10-24-diag-11b-instruction-counts.md); (1a), (3) and (5)): `diag-overhead.py instructions` runs both binaries' off mode under Valgrind's cachegrind at two frame counts and judges the instructions per frame, which the layout does not change (`test-diag-overhead.py` covers it; teeth proved). linux-gcc-shipping against a scratch configuration of it with profiling on: offline +0.08%, jobs +0.00% (four threads -0.23% .. +0.23%), within 1%; alloc +5.55%, the reported worst case; the extra work is the statistics gate (7.8 to 8.5 instructions each), so offline's Windows residual is the layout floor, not work. The same pair's timing boards are within on offline and jobs (frame time and CPU per frame). Windows keeps the exact count times cost: no unprivileged instruction counter on this host, an MSVC count being DIAG.11c open qualification. **(2) closed 2026-10-24** ([session](sessions/2026-10-24-diag-11b-basic-allocator-watch.md)): basic's +4.0% to +5.7% on allocation-heavy frames was the allocator watch's forwarding (99.3% of its extra instructions); the watch now observes the allocator's refusals (`IRefusalObserver`, every engine allocator reports through `IAllocator::refuse()`, before its fatal), and basic reads +0.07% to +0.47% (frame and CPU), within 2% on all three tuples; per lane, `ci-evidence.py compare` on hosted run 39239067340 (a median test 2.2, 2.7 and at most 2.6 times; teeth); the board found two fiber annotation leaks, now fixed: ASan mapped a fake stack per job (linux-gcc-asan jobs 24.3 times and 4.5 GiB, now 1.54 times and 132 MiB) and each job left a TSan frame on its fiber's shadow stack (io 955 MiB after 3600 frames, now 41 MiB), each held by a new case with teeth; the audit of that run: every sanitizer lane executed its tests, the 13 corpus replays and its negative controls (5 or 6 of 11 seeds detected, the rest not applicable), GenMC found both seeded defects, and `host-check.yml` (dispatch-only) last succeeded in 38605464765; `--check` fails any error, warning or lost message), `diag-overhead.py` reports `validation-*` arms against off, and `create_dx12_raster_context` takes the validation request; core costs +22.7% frame and +40.0% CPU on the D3D12 hardware adapter, +7.5% and +2.1% on lavapipe, synchronization +11.6% and +2.9%; three defects found and fixed, each with teeth: the authored forward graph's D3D12 output clear mismatched its target every frame (820/821; `FramePlans::output_clear`, `SceneRenderer::output_clear()`, `create_color_depth_target_for`, used by the harness, the sandbox and the gizmo probe), turning GBV on beside a live D3D12 device failed creation with `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_RESET` (`SettledByLiveDevice`; the f-3 case no longer skips its plain leg), and overlapping copies in one Vulkan upload batch raced (`WRITE_AFTER_WRITE`; a transfer barrier before an overlapping copy); hosted once pushed: every lane passes `crd-diag-overhead-render-validation-core` (WARP with the debug layer, lavapipe with the pinned layer) and the Linux lanes `-sync`, the Windows lanes the f-3 case with its three new legs, and every lane the frame-cook output-clear case and the Vulkan suites. **Hosted flakes, 2026-10-24** ([session](sessions/2026-10-24-diag-11b-hosted-flaky-readings.md)): a jobs pressure reading showed a high water below its count (every reading now reports at least the count, with the lifetime ledger's `live`), the ring tearing case could end before its reader copied (the writer now laps until it has), and a released reservation read as mapped under TSan because the runtime maps the freed range (`os_range_no_access` judges releases; 10,000 TSan runs clean), each with teeth; hosted once pushed: the four cases pass on every lane. **The overlapping batch uploads** (2026-10-24; [session](sessions/2026-10-24-diag-11b-batch-upload-overlap.md)): a run that moved on consecutive syncs was queued twice and uploaded its four sections twice, the overlapping copies of one Vulkan batch; `SceneRenderer::sync` queues a run once (`RunEntry::queued`), and the batch barrier has a case of its own under synchronization validation, each with teeth; hosted once pushed: the scene-render case passes on every lane, the Vulkan case on every lane with a Vulkan device (lavapipe on Linux), and the Linux lanes' `-sync` stays clean. **The standalone targets' clear values** (2026-10-24; [session](sessions/2026-10-24-diag-11b-standalone-target-clears.md)): a case draws into every standalone D3D12 kind at the default clear under the debug layer's capture; the MSAA, G-buffer and visibility targets had no clear value (820 on every clear) and are created for the default (opaque black, id 0), and a depth PSO was cached without its DSV format, so a D24S8 draw bound the D32 pipeline (615); the format joins the cache identity (teeth: both restored fail the case). Hosted once pushed: the Windows lanes pass the case on WARP and crd-gpu-context-dx12-tests. **D3D12 GPU-based validation** (2026-10-24; [session](sessions/2026-10-24-diag-11b-gpu-based-validation.md)): its crash was ours: `frame_prepare` bound a null root signature at each frame's first heap reservation, and the second frame's injected readback barrier faulted in the NVIDIA driver; it now binds an empty signature, which drops the tables as before, and the harness capture holds 4,096 records (GBV's infos overflowed 256); the cost is +66.1% frame time and +227.7% CPU per frame on win-shipping ([board](bench/2026-10-24-diag-11b-device-validation.md)); teeth: the null bind crashes and the old capacity fails `crd-diag-overhead-render-validation-gpu`. The notice reports the instrumentation, not API misuse, so the Vulkan capture counts it as `ValidationReport::cost_notices` (a warning only; an error under that name still counts) and `--check` reports it; robustness was not forced, so every check runs. GPU-assisted validation costs +80.3% frame time and +129.4% CPU per frame on lavapipe ([board](bench/2026-10-24-diag-11b-device-validation.md)). Three `[bench-diag]` cases in `crd-bench` time each mechanism per call against its own baseline in one binary, each checking its answers first: a generation check adds 0.5 to 0.7 ns per lookup over a raw index (an entity check about 0.55 ns); a traced run costs +35% to +55% over a plain run with `replay.record`'s 4,096 events kept and +137% to +232% with all 60,004, the encode about 45 ns per kept event; a float-mode boundary 14 ns on MSVC and 3.5 ns on GCC, a value probe 1.3 to 2.4 ns per value, a pressure or ledger snapshot 10 to 60 ns, a ledger record 15 ns (win-release and linux-gcc-relwithdebinfo). The WSL host's tidy is 20.1.0, Windows' and the hosted lanes' 20.1.8; the gate checks the major only. `wsl-lane.sh dev linux-gcc-debug` on the two resources test sources now ends passed (169 CTests, both guards, tidy clean with 20.1.0) where it ended incomplete; `tidy-files.py` on `crdr.cpp`, `test_cook.cpp` and `fuzz_resource_reload.cpp` is clean with 20.1.0 on linux-gcc-shipping and with 20.1.8 on win-debug: no version-dependent finding. Hosted once pushed: no Linux job prints zstd's configure deprecation, every lane builds zstd 1.5.7 with no warning, applies the two patches and passes crd-resources-tests and the corpus replay, and the repository job passes check-pins, the license manifest check and the new resolver and installer tests on Windows and Linux. **External sampling judged; C++ Build Insights read** (2026-10-25; [session](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-11b-external-sampling.md), [board](bench/2026-10-25-diag-11b-external-sampling.md), [build board](bench/2026-10-18-build-speed-compile-time.md#c-build-insights-capture-2026-10-25)): clause (8). The user's elevated capture of 2026-10-25 (Visual Studio closed) read six DISAGREES. Each was explained from the trace (per-function samples, sampled addresses and cdb on the kept PDB) and two boards: (a) off over compiled-out on alloc, jobs and offline: off runs no out-of-line diagnostic code (0.0 to 0.1% of compiled-out's CPU), so judging the two binaries' frame time against the diagnostic share was mis-specified; the gaps are alloc's force-inlined statistics gate in `TlsfAllocator::try_allocate` (91% of +8.4% sampled CPU; clause (1)'s worst case, not layout), jobs' contended fiber pool (`FiberPool::acquire` +11.8%) and offline's layout (`run_seq` -5.1%); the pair was also built from two sources (the baseline 6 h newer, with `d7836fbb`'s strings). A two-binary row is now judged on the diagnostic code off runs (within 2% of none) and the gap located; counting `try_allocate` or `FiberPool::acquire` as diagnostic makes the trace's rows disagree. (b) jobs full -14.0%: `record` had run the harness's default eight threads on 16 logical processors, where off spends 53% of its CPU in `FiberPool::acquire` (37% on the free-list head's `lock cmpxchg`) and full's per-job work relieves it; CPU falls too (-7.5% sampled, -8.25% by the harness at that shape) while the diagnostic frames add 11.6% and their call sites 8.6%; at the boards' four threads on `5555` full reads +37.1% frame and +37.0% CPU (reported; no budget hides it). `record` now runs the boards' shape and keeps the CPU measurand; a row is measured on CPU against CPU, on frame time only for one-thread frames, a cell's processes must agree within 2% (jobs basic's read 393 and 471 µs: unresolved), and the C runtime's `snprintf` and copies run for their caller. (c) offline full +258.2%: the classes were right and the estimate wrong (it divided by full's own non-diagnostic samples); the bridge's hooks are +202.3% of off's CPU (`adapter_pre` +116.4%, `adapter_post` +85.5%, the `rdtsc` reads inlined in them), the executor's call sites around them in `run_seq` +43.9% (reloads after each `call rax`), elsewhere +6.5%; the added CPU must now sit in the diagnostic frames and their call sites, and the trace AGREES. `record` refuses a harness tree with work left before the sampler starts (repaired the same day: a plain `ninja -n` never passes a CONFIGURE_DEPENDS tree, whose dry run stops at the always-dirty glob check before it looks at the target; `tree_current` now runs the glob check for real and dry-runs build.ninja and the target past that edge, with real-tree teeth). Re-analysed (`analyze`, exit 6): ten rows agree, jobs basic unresolved, jobs full disagrees (its frame is the pool's contention at eight threads). `test-sample-overhead-wpr.py` 12 → 18 cases; 13 mutations each fail it. The fiber pool's contended free list is owned by [JOBS-HARD.3](#slice-jobs-hard.3), fixed there the same day (kept fibers, one wake per batch; [session](sessions/2026-10-25-jobs-fiber-pool-contention.md)): the jobs rows are re-recorded by the user's next sampling run. This trace's jobs off-over-compiled-out gap (`FiberPool::acquire` +11.8%) and jobs full's eight-thread DISAGREES were the pool; the jobs frame is now 40% to 89% cheaper on Windows, so the boards measured before it no longer describe the jobs workload, and jobs full is expected to read its own cost at every shape. C++ Build Insights (clause closed): win-debug, PCH off, uncached, 176.5 s, front end 1,705.5 s, back end 71.7 s, include parsing 1,297.8 s; Catch2's test macros 243.5 s over 1,002 units, MSVC's stream headers 191 s over 1,273 (Catch2's `catch_tostring.hpp` in 601 units, `crd/math/complex.hpp`'s `<complex>` in 547), `cmath.hpp` 172.7 s over 644; function generation 135.6 s, 126.8 s of it the FFT codelets generated again in each of 21 units; vcperf's template table was empty, so template time is read from `/d1reportTime`. Follow-up data, not started: the codelets in one unit, one fuzz replay driver for the 13 targets (about 19 s of edges), a `cmath.hpp` without `<complex>`. Hosted once pushed: the repository jobs pass "Overhead external sampling verdicts" with the 18 cases and every Ninja lane passes `crd-check-diag-runbooks`. **Tracy Profiler as the peer on comparable features** (2026-10-26; [session](sessions/2026-10-26-diag-11b-tracy-peer.md), [board](bench/2026-10-26-diag-11b-tracy-peer.md)): clause (8)'s peer part, closed. Tracy 0.14.1 (the user's choice and approval; the operator's download) is pinned (`packages/tracy`, BSD-3-Clause, gated by the new `CRD_DIAG_PEER_TRACY`, off in every preset and lane; `tools/tracy-capture-windows` and `tools/tracy-csvexport-windows` with member digests), its client built from a build-owned copy with two registered patches for the three real defects GCC 13 reported in it (an unchecked `pipe()`, unbounded host-description writes, an unchecked `fscanf`). `crd-diag-overhead-tracy` holds Cerid's modes and Tracy's counterparts at the same sites in one binary (`DOV_SITE`, a zone per job through crd-jobs' observer, a zone per CEIR op through `RunHooks`, `FrameMark`, `TracyAllocN` with `--memory`); `scripts/diag-overhead.py` runs `tracy` (no client) and `tracy-connected` (a verified `tracy-capture` per process, the capture held to every zone, frame and message). The mapping: zones, frame marks (partial: Cerid's also snapshots the history), kept sites, the jobs adapter, the CEIR bridge (closest: Tracy derives the aggregate from a zone per op), counters, allocator statistics (partial: Tracy logs events) have peers; basic's filter, the allocator watch, crash capture, triggers and bundles, replay, device validation and the host frame have none; GPU scopes have one, not measured. Before the repairs Cerid lost a zone +7.1 ns, a mark +70.3 ns and a kept site +4.6 ns on Windows; a Tracy build on `steady_clock` put the zone's whole gap on the clock. Repaired: crd-time's `TickClock` (MonotonicClock's timeline from the cycle counter, calibrated, re-anchored, never stepping back) for zones, marks and `diagnostic_now_ns`; the frame mark writes its slot directly with single-writer counters (−65.4 ns); a stopped profiler's entry points no longer take the lifetime pin and a zone with no profiler makes no call; and a latent bug, a `CRD_PERF_SCOPE` site first reached before `cp::init` was unnamed for good (`detail::SiteName`). Now on Windows (connected / no client): a zone +1.4 ns over Tracy's on the final board (−0.8 to −0.5 ns on three earlier builds of nearly the same source: build-to-build layout), a kept site +1.2 to +3.9 ns and 55 to 66 cycles less CPU, a mark +4.3 ns (the lifetime pin and history); disabled sites, zones and marks at or below Tracy's no-client cost; offline −41.7% frame and −70.5% CPU, allocator totals 9.4 ns a pair against Tracy's 65.5. On linux-gcc-shipping (zones and marks compiled out) a kept site −4.6 ns and 354 fewer instructions, jobs −3.3% frame and −19.9% CPU. Teeth: each clock set back fails `[diag11b]`; the old site static fails the late-start name case; `test-diag-overhead.py` 22 → 29 cases; TickClock's cases under TSan 20 times. Follow-up (q), a design decision, the loss: at four jobs threads on Windows Cerid's full frame is +15.9% (+14.6 .. +17.3) over Tracy connected on the final board (+4.8% and +12.6% on two earlier boards of the same workload before the last source changes); Cerid's kept site per job costs 71.8 µs a frame against Tracy's 50.6 (its jobs adapter is cheaper than Tracy's zone per job, 26.0 against 36.6), put by experiment on the flight recorder's one claim word and shared slots (thread-private claims and slots: 45.5 µs); removing them changes the recorder's single total order and the trigger window's exactness (ADR-0133). The user decided C2 (2026-10-26) and **(q1) is done** (2026-10-26, [session](sessions/2026-10-26-flight-recorder-c2.md), [board](bench/2026-10-26-diag-11b-flight-recorder-c2.md), ADR-0133 ID-7; [decision brief](sessions/2026-10-26-flight-recorder-per-thread-rings-decision.md)): one global sequence by one `fetch_add` and per-thread rings (64 rings of `capacity`, at most 65,536 events together, and a shared ring for threads without storage; 9.65 MiB at the default policy, resident growth unchanged); void claims are given back so the sequences stay dense and CFR1 is unchanged; a capture places every ring's events at their sequence; threads take a ring on their first kept event and give it back at exit. Proof: new `test_diag_flight_rings.cpp` cases (causal order of a token passed between threads, the trigger boundary in the trigger's own thread, exactly `post_events` with void claims given back, late and exiting threads, rings without storage and 70 live threads, a reload under load, a refused allocation); teeth: a merge by timestamp, rings concatenated, the boundary one claim late and a claim past the window kept each fail them. Two defects found and fixed: the live-recorder list's spinlock hung 70 exiting threads under TSan (now a never-destroyed mutex), and the tests' own waits starved writers under TSan. win-debug, win-asan, linux-gcc-debug, linux-gcc-asan and linux-clang-tsan (5 repeats and 5 pinned beside busy loops) pass. Measured: four-thread jobs full **−4.20% (−5.12 .. −3.28) against Tracy connected**, CPU −7.82%, and −5.79% against the global ring (30 processes); basic within 2% on jobs, alloc and offline; the disabled budget within 1% on instructions. The coverage registry's two out-of-memory exclusions are removed (a test-only seam produces them). Needs CI: the ring cases on every hosted lane and the coverage measurement. Push run 40340291698 (`4e0a0298`) failed two ring cases on seven lanes, both the tests' own races (workers that finished before the trigger, stats read after the threads exited), now fixed (2026-10-26, [session](sessions/2026-10-26-ci-audit-and-flight-ring-races.md)). The C2 batch's one SEGV was a real fault whose address Catch2's re-raise had replaced with `{pid, uid}`; a fault in a test case is now reported as itself, and the fault was this host's misexecution, which a Cerid-free control of the same loop shows too (2026-10-26, [session](sessions/2026-10-26-catch2-fault-address.md)). The +2.9% the cycle-counter clock cost that frame over `steady_clock` in one binary is explained and fixed (2026-10-26, [session](sessions/2026-10-26-diag-11b-tick-clock-inlining.md), [board](bench/2026-10-26-diag-11b-tick-clock-inlining.md)): MSVC LTCG left `std::atomic`'s loads out of line in TickClock's mapped read, six calls, because the harness's `/arch:AVX2` units supplied those inline bodies and LTCG does not inline an AVX2 body into baseline-ISA crd-time (a two-file probe reproduces it by link order); at four threads the read's latency reaches the frame. The read now loads through the compiler's own operations; `TickClock::prefer_system()` and the harness's `--clock system` make the system clock an arm of the same binary, and `diag-overhead.py` judges `tick-clock` (full over `full-system-clock`) at 0%: jobs −2.22% (−4.35 .. −1.29), within; teeth, the read back on `std::atomic`'s loads, +4.16% (+2.86 .. +5.89), over; linux-gcc-shipping (inlined before and after) −1.22% (−2.14 .. −0.04), within; a kept site 6.2 ns and a zone 12.7 ns cheaper than on the system clock. The hosted windows-shipping step back of complete run 40274818980 (`TickClock: no thread's reads step back while any thread re-anchors`, `1 == 0`) fits the hand-off to the first map, which stepped back in 8 of 300 local processes (per-vCPU counter offsets on the hosted VM not reproduced, not ruled out): the publisher read its pair before taking the state and other readers kept returning MonotonicClock readings past its anchor (QueryPerformanceCounter's 100 ns grid); fixed 2026-10-26 ([session](sessions/2026-10-26-tick-clock-cross-thread.md), [board](bench/2026-10-26-diag-11b-tick-clock-hand-off.md)): the publisher takes the state first and anchors at or above a floor every returned reading raised, readers meeting a publication wait for it; the contract is per-thread monotonic, not a happens-before order; new migration and many-hand-off cases with teeth (11 of 300); jobs full vs full-system-clock −2.49% (−4.63 .. −1.76), within. Needs CI: the three cross-thread cases on every hosted lane. Follow-up (r), a design decision awaiting the user: the same mixed-ISA LTCG defect reaches every baseline-ISA library an AVX2 unit links before (crd-perf's `pop_region`, striped counts and jobs adapter among them; an experiment with crd-time and crd-perf at `/arch:AVX2` read the full jobs frame at 311 against 341 µs, across binaries); its fix, one ISA level for the whole program, changes every library's code generation, the reach of crd-simd-flags (GCC's `-mfma`, ADR-0063's determinism options) and the relocatable package's `Cerid::simd-flags`. | [DIAG.11a](#slice-diag.11a); [SIMD-DISPATCH](#slice-simd-dispatch) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-11b); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md) |

Fixed: no case checked DIAG.0's missing symbolizer or DIAG.3f's symbolized site; a sanitizer catch now names the specimen's site (`Expectation::expected_site`), and a report with no symbolized frame is `MissingSymbolizer` (seed `asan-symbolizer-off`, DETECTED on win-asan and linux-gcc-asan); crd-core's assert-handler re-entrancy guard, which out of memory outside a sink relies on, has a case. Owned: the initialized-memory route and the default heap's out of memory (MEM-HARD.2), scoped static lifetime (MEM-HARD.1), an out-of-process collector (OPS.1), the N-1 reader (OPS.2), Windows fiber-stack faults (JOBS-HARD.1), Linux unloaded modules (EXT.3), MCP cancellation (AGENT.2), the window's buttons (I2D-9.e), rr (QUAL.2), dSYM and WASM identities (MAC.DIAG, WEB.DIAG); teeth on win-debug and win-asan. Hosted once pushed: the four sanitizer cases pass on every lane, the seed is DETECTED on win-asan and linux-gcc-asan, and crd-core-tests passes the re-entrancy case on every lane. **The asset reader on the I/O seam** (2026-10-25, [session](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-10b-asset-read-seam.md)): `read_diag_asset_file` consults the I/O fault seam as a `ReadWhole` before it opens the file, so a failed, short or cancelled read is `DiagAssetRead::Failed` and a `failed-read` plan fails a host profile, flight policy or `fault.arm` asset read; the coverage exclusion is deleted; teeth on win-debug. Hosted once pushed: the `diag assets:` and `fault commands:` cases pass on every lane. **UBSan and use-after-scope** (2026-10-25, [session](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-3f-ubsan-use-after-scope.md)): DIAG.3f's two unclaimed classes have specimens; `stack-use-after-scope` caught on win-asan and linux-gcc-asan, the signed overflow by UBSan on linux-gcc-asan (`LanePromise::ubsan`), each naming its site; both activation seeds strip them; teeth on win-asan and linux-gcc-asan. Hosted once pushed: the two `diag 3f:` cases pass on every lane (caught where promised, absent elsewhere), and the two activation seeds stay DETECTED on win-asan and linux-gcc-asan. **DIAG.5b's SIGFPE, injection and ASan records** (2026-10-25, [session](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-5b-fpe-injection-asan.md)): mode `fpe` (a trapped floating-point division) records signal 8, `FPE_FLTDIV`; the Linux record writer honours the `DumpFailed` injection and the hook's marker shows it; on linux-gcc-asan, measured first, UBSan stops the null-store modes (exit 1, no record) and the kernel-raised faults are recorded, then reported by ASan. Found and fixed: crd re-raised a fault after recording it, so a chained handler (ASan) got a `tkill` siginfo and reported a made-up address; a kernel-raised fault now returns and re-executes under the previous handler, and a sent signal is still re-raised; a sent signal's negative `si_code` is written signed. The Linux cases judge a sanitizer by `lane_promise()`; teeth on linux-gcc-debug and linux-gcc-asan. Hosted once pushed: the Linux `crash capture`, `crash record` and host crash cases pass on every Linux lane with the linux-gcc-asan expectations, the Windows `[crash-capture]` cases on win-debug and win-asan, and seed `linux-crash-record-truncated` stays DETECTED. **A Windows dump of a headless fatal** (2026-10-25, [session](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-5c-headless-fatal-dump.md)): `install()` registers a SIGABRT handler on Windows (UCRT's `abort()` calls it and never reaches the unhandled-exception filter); it re-arms itself, dumps the aborting thread through the fatal path with `kAbortExceptionCode` (`STATUS_FATAL_APP_EXIT`) and returns, so `abort()` still exits 3; every `abort()` takes the route; measured on win-asan as on win-debug; teeth on win-debug. Hosted once pushed: the crash-capture abort case passes with one dump on every Windows lane, and the host's `invariant`, `assert` and `use-after-free` cases with one dump on every Windows lane outside ASan. **Needs CI** (local work done 2026-10-25): run 40140275224 (head `01562de5`, running) carries the clauses up to the asset reader; complete-tier run [40150944943](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/40150944943) (head `b0f112ad`) failed 16 jobs on three defects, now repaired ([session](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-final-ci-repairs.md): a test's 8.3 temp path, a helper unused without asserts on Linux, an LSan seed naming a renamed site); the next complete-tier run must show every "Hosted once pushed" clause of this row on its lanes, and the re-read's published-CI half reads that run. **Published-CI half** (2026-10-26, [session](sessions/2026-10-26-ci-audit-and-flight-ring-races.md)): complete-tier run 40307186916 at `ae7bdc23` passes every hosted test the DIAG rows name (skips only where the rows say: Vulkan on hosted Windows, the window cases on headless runners) and every seed is DETECTED; it predates C2, so the final complete-tier run after C2 must show the flight-recorder clauses (the restricted-record case, `crd-fuzz-perf-flight-corpus`, the ring cases) again. **Fault addresses** (2026-10-26, [session](sessions/2026-10-26-catch2-fault-address.md)): Catch2's POSIX handler re-raised every fault, so sanitizers reported a fault in a test case at the sender's `{pid, uid}`; the `catch2-posix-signal-refault` patch lets a kernel fault re-execute under the restored handler, ThreadSanitizer trees compile no Catch2 signal handler, and `guard_current_thread_stack` reads the thread's alternate stack from the kernel each time (a borrowed stack its owner took away left none); teeth on linux-gcc-asan and linux-clang-tsan. Hosted once pushed: the two `catch2 fault:` cases pass on every Linux lane and the wild-read case on every Windows lane, and the borrowed-stack contract case on every Linux lane. | [DIAG.11c](#slice-diag.11c) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-12); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Re-read](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-12-re-read.md); [Owners](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-12-owner-rows.md); [Headless fatal](sessions/2026-10-25-diag-5c-headless-fatal-dump.md); [Run](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/40140275224) |

| 602 | <a id="slice-jobs-hard.3"></a>**JOBS-HARD.3** | Later | Full task/allocator/CEIR consumer board: actual pool utilization, oversubscription, single-worker progress and cross-platform soak. Reuse DIAG mechanisms; qualify broader/new product consumers and targets here. Also owned (2026-10-25, [DIAG.11b](#slice-diag.11b) clause (8)): the fiber pool's shared free list contended. In the overhead harness's tiny-job workload (2048 jobs a frame, eight threads on 16 logical processors) `FiberPool::acquire` held 53% of the off arm's sampled CPU, 37% on the instruction after the Treiber head's `lock cmpxchg` ([board](bench/2026-10-25-diag-11b-external-sampling.md)). **Fixed the same day** (1a12466c, e0de26cc, cdcc3e79; [session](sessions/2026-10-25-jobs-fiber-pool-contention.md), [bench](bench/2026-10-25-jobs-fiber-pool-contention.md), [ADR-0033](decisions/0033-jobs-implementation-architecture.md) decisions 8 and 9): a thread keeps its completed job's fiber, still acquired, for its next new job of the same tier, and returns it before a resume, before a job of another tier, just before it blocks waiting for work, when it leaves its loop, when the main thread's `wait()` or pump returns and at shutdown, so the pool's readings stay exact and the sizing bound (parked fibers plus threads) is unchanged; an idle-fiber cache was rejected because its high water cannot be exact without a global update per acquire. `jobs::run` of a span now wakes once per batch with at most one token per thread: the token and wake per job had left up to 2048 tokens a frame for idle workers to spin through and cost a futex syscall per job, which made linux-gcc-shipping's off 25% slower once the pool no longer paced the consumers. The free list is reached about once per thread per frame (203, 207 and 50 to 124 acquisitions for 409,600 jobs at four, eight and sixteen threads). Against the build before: win-shipping-profile off -39.9% frame and -42.4% CPU at four threads on 5555, -50.2% / -67.0% at eight and -88.6% / -86.2% at sixteen; linux-gcc-shipping's profiling twin -46.1% / -57.1%, -46.8% / -68.6% and -98.0% / -87.6%; basic and full alike; cachegrind -13.0% instructions a frame at one thread and -56% at four; off over compiled-out +0.00% in instructions. Correct on win-debug, win-asan, linux-gcc-debug, linux-gcc-asan and linux-clang-tsan, repeated and under a pinned busy-loop neighbour, with new cases and teeth (`test_fiber_keep.cpp`, `test_batch_wake.cpp`). Outside readers no longer race a restart (0e1433ef): every snapshot pins the pool, `shutdown()` closes it and waits for pinned readers before freeing anything, a closed pool reads as one before `init()`, and `test_diag_snapshot_restart.cpp` crashed four of four runs on win-asan and on win-debug with the old guard. The streaming case's remaining linux-clang-tsan fault is this host's: values no store wrote (a `{pid, uid}` word, instruction bytes, a null `unique_ptr::get()` of a held array), no signal before it, and a Cerid-free control of the same shape faults too, also with no fiber at all (300 interleaved rounds: the case 41, the fiber control 30, the plain control 24; the plain control unsanitized 0 of 250 beside 5 of 250 under TSan); hosted linux-clang-tsan passed the case. `host-check.yml` gained two dispatch-only TSan legs (`scripts/tsan-host-check.py`, 150 pinned rounds each of the case and the control's three arms) that settle it on hosted machines. **Fewer processors than threads** (2026-10-26, [session](sessions/2026-10-26-jobs-fiber-keep-oversubscription.md)): complete run 40274818980 failed the short-frames case on the hosted 4-vCPU Windows runner (17,524 acquisitions for 25,600 jobs, 0.68 a job; the 140,192 in the check is that times 8). The hook fired only before real sleeps; a pusher slower than the workers made them sleep between nearly every two jobs. A worker that keeps a fiber now spins for a token (128 turns of pauses and a yield, a read of the count, the protocol unchanged) before it gives the fiber back: 50 to 67 acquisitions at one to eight processors on win-debug, 53 to 179 at two to eight on linux-gcc-debug and 567 to 831 at one (bound 3,200; teeth 8 of 8 on both); a new case pins the pool to one and two processors itself. win-shipping-profile jobs board neutral at four and eight threads, -12.8% frame and +17.6% CPU at four threads on two processors; the case is 1.6x slower on linux-gcc-debug at one processor (recorded trade). Left here: the dispatched host check, and the scheduler's single shared injection queue under one producer, which this did not measure beyond the jobs workload. | [JOBS-HARD.2](#slice-jobs-hard.2) | [Detail](research/2026-09-12-cerid-whole-system-review.md#finding-g03); [ADR-0130](decisions/0130-system-qualification-and-agent-driven-products.md); [G03](research/2026-09-12-cerid-whole-system-review.md#finding-g03); [Diagnostic foundation](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#existing-programme-ownership-after-diag) |

## Verification and maintenance

Check this document with `python scripts/check-master-plan.py` from the repository root. It verifies the single
table, order, IDs, prerequisites, source routing and current documentation links; it does not test engine features.

Per slice: fulfill the linked contract, test the actual asset/runtime path, preserve last-good reload and record
quality/performance evidence. Local work uses changed modules plus blast radius, scoped CTest and incremental tidy;
whole-repository/configuration sweeps belong to CI. [BUILDING](BUILDING.md), [PRINCIPLES](PRINCIPLES.md) and
[SANITY](SANITY.md) remain binding. No performance claims without a saved full board in [bench](bench/README.md).

Update a row once with its evidence link; update a parent only when every child passes. Keep current focus as one
pointer in context. Sessions, ADRs, recipes, research and archives preserve knowledge; do not copy live Next lists
into them. A finding in the audit is assigned to a row here, not a new competing backlog. The user owns commits.
